Exact family and new conclusion\n\nRetain the ORIGINAL physical word and every query, writing\n\n    h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2),\n    S_j=sum block_j, j=0,1,2, b>1, d>1.\n\nEach block is a possibly empty list of nonnegative physical steps.\nThe following symbols are local abbreviations, not changes to B_n:\n\n    T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1, N=1+S_2, sigma=2N-1,\n    A=b-1, E=d-1,\n    u>=0, v>0, ell>0, alpha>=0, beta>=0, kappa>=0,\n    delta=A-Mu-alpha, C=Eu-alpha, Z=Ev-beta-Nell.\n\nIn the real plane with its usual orthonormal basis e_1,e_2 put\n\n    p=e_1, q=-u e_1-v e_2, r=ell e_2,\n    J(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},\n    F(x)=min_y {J(y)+||x-y||^2/2},\n    X_0=(T+delta)e_1-(Mv+beta)e_2.                  (1)\n\nThe declared family consists of ALL these parameters satisfying\n\n    delta>=0,\n    v(Mv+beta)<=(1+u)delta+kappa,\n    kappa<=u alpha+v beta,\n    ell Z>=max{0,C,-u C-v Z-kappa}.                 (2)\n\nThese are exactly the conditions for gradient p on block_0 and its\ncheckpoint, q on block_1 and its checkpoint, and r on block_2 including\nthe actual terminal query, for the single static function (1).\nIndependent alpha,beta include the complete C102 nonradial interface.\n\nThe true closest minimizer, squared distance, and payoff are\n\n    x_star=(0,-min{Mv+beta,kappa/v}),\n    R_nr^2=(T+delta)^2+(Mv+beta-kappa/v)_+^2,\n    V_nr=2F(X_n)/R_nr^2\n        =[2ell(Ev-beta)-sigma ell^2]/R_nr^2.        (3)\n\nHere t_+=max(t,0); the symbol R_nr is a distance and is unrelated to the\nproject's convention W=2R. Multiplying F by any L>0 preserves (3) under\nthe original steps h_i/L.\n\nThe new general word-conditioned bound is: if\n\n    T>=sqrt(2) A,                                  (4)\n\nthen EVERY member of (1)--(2) satisfies\n\n    V_nr <= max{ E^2/[sigma(T+A+M)^2],\n                 (AE/(TM))^2/sigma }.             (5)\n\nThe first term is the accepted single-cut witness at the SECOND actual\ncheckpoint. The second term retains the actual first, middle and tail\nblock masses. No parameter optimum is left undecided in the comparison.\nAn intermediate scalar relaxation below is not asserted to be attained\nby a legal homogeneous instance.\n\nFor the ORIGINAL enclosing-m ruler use, throughout every child word,\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_m=1-1/m,\n    c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,\n    H_0=(), H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a_m c_m^k,H_k),\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, M_k=1+sum H_k.\n\nThe local beta in (1) is NOT beta_m. For every m>=128, every pair of\nactual checkpoint indices satisfies (4), and\n\n    sup_(all parameters (2), all pairs on H_m) V_nr\n       <= gamma103/(2M_m-1)\n        = gamma103 K_n(H_m)\n        = gamma103 F_ray_n(H_m),\n    gamma103=5949/6088<1,                           (6)\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V_nr\n       <=2gamma103/exp(1)<1.                       (7)\n\nThus even the ENTIRE independent nonradial-middle family is weaker than\nthe complete same-word Huber/ray reference. The Huber-augmented complete\napplicable lens reference also dominates it. This is not an upper bound\non unrestricted W_n(H_m). A distinct endpoint-angle construction is\ngiven in Section12; its profile comparison is not claimed solved.\n\n## 2. The one static function, actual minimum and radius\n\nJ is a finite continuous convex nonnegative maximum of affine functions.\nFor fixed x its defining proximal objective is coercive and strictly\nconvex, so it has a unique minimizer y(x). Its optimality condition is\n\n    g(x):=x-y(x) in partial J(y(x)).                (8)\n\nFor completeness, a minimizer compared to y+t(z-y), convexity of J,\nand division by t>0 followed by t down to0 give the subgradient\ninequality J(z)>=J(y)+<x-y,z-y>. Conversely that inequality and the\nquadratic expansion imply that the objective at z exceeds its value\nat y by at least ||z-y||^2/2, proving (8).\n\nAdd the two subgradient inequalities at x,x'. With g=g(x),g'=g(x'),\none obtains <g-g',y-y'>>=0 and hence\n\n    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.\n\nThus g is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the proximal objective for x' at y\ngives the upper bound in\n\n    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<g,x'-x><=||x'-x||^2/2.\n\nFor the lower bound, apply the subgradient inequality at y to y' and\nexpand the two quadratic terms; the remainder is ||g'-g||^2/2.\nThese inequalities prove differentiability with gradient g and convexity\nby the supporting affine function at each x. Therefore F is everywhere\ndefined, convex and1-smooth.\n\nIts minimum is0 and its minimizer set is exactly\n\n    K={x_1<=0,x_2<=0,-u x_1-v x_2<=kappa}.         (9)\n\nIndeed J is zero precisely there, and F(x)=0 forces both nonnegative\nsummands at the attained minimum to vanish. For u>0, K is the triangle\nwith vertices(0,0),(-kappa/u,0),(0,-kappa/v), allowing degeneracy.\nFor u=0 it is the strip x_1<=0,-kappa/v<=x_2<=0.\n\nEvery point of K has x_1<=0 and -kappa/v<=x_2<=0. The stated x_star\nlies in K and separately minimizes the two coordinate-square distances\nfrom X_0, whose first coordinate is positive and second is negative.\nIt is therefore the true unique closest minimizer. This proves both\npositive-part branches in (3); no old uncentered norm is substituted.\n\n## 3. All physical gradients and all original directed rows\n\nGive the four slopes p,q,r,0 the affine costs0,kappa,0,0. If slope g_i\nis active at y_i=x_i-g_i, then (8) gives its actual gradient and\n\n    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i.\n\nFor every ordered pair of such samples the residual from accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290 expands to\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>-k_i+k_j<=0.                 (10)\n\nThe last inequality is exactly activity of j's plane against i's at\ny_j. It proves BOTH orientations of every remote row, not merely\nadjacent or checkpoint rows. Include the optimum sample with position\nx_star and g_star=f_star=k_star=0, so y_star=x_star: the zero plane is\nactive there and the same argument proves both optimum rows.\n\nAt a physical prefix cumulative mass t in[0,S_0], the point and its\nproposed proximal point are\n\n    x=(T+delta-t,-Mv-beta),\n    y=(delta+S_0-t,-Mv-beta).\n\nAt the last prefix query p's plane dominates zero and q's plane exactly\nby the first two inequalities in (2); r's plane is negative. Earlier\nqueries add a nonnegative multiple of p, improving all comparisons\nsince p's directional slope1 exceeds the others0,-u,0.\n\nThe first checkpoint sends the point to(-Mu-alpha,-Mv-beta). At middle\ncumulative mass t in[0,S_1],\n\n    x=(-(M-t)u-alpha,-(M-t)v-beta),\n    y=(-alpha-(S_1-t)u,-beta-(S_1-t)v).\n\nAt t=S_1 the q-plane height is u alpha+v beta-kappa>=0, while p,r\nhave heights-alpha,-ell beta. Earlier queries add a nonnegative multiple\nof q; its directional slope u^2+v^2 exceeds0,-u,-ell v. Thus every\nmiddle query is legal.\n\nThe second checkpoint sends the point to(C,Ev-beta). At tail cumulative\nmass t in[0,S_2], its proposed proximal point is\n\n    y=(C,Ev-beta-(1+t)ell).\n\nAt the actual terminal query t=S_2, (2) compares r's plane against all\nthree competitors. Earlier tail queries add a nonnegative multiple of\ne_2, improving every comparison. This proves the complete physical\ntrajectory and (10), including large ordinary steps and zero steps.\n\nNecessity also holds: p,q,r are exposed vertices of the slope hull,\nexposed respectively by e_1,-e_2,e_2. A subgradient of a finite affine\nmaximum is a convex combination of its active slopes: if it were not,\na separating direction would contradict the directional derivative,\nwhich is the largest active directional slope. Thus an exposed slope\nas the actual gradient in (8) requires its own plane to be active.\nThe three ending-query comparisons give precisely (2).\n\nAt the terminal query the active-plane value formula gives\nf_n=ell(Ev-beta)-(S_2+1/2)ell^2, proving (3). It is positive because\nZ>=0 implies Ev-beta>=Nell and hence its numerator is at least ell^2.\n\n## 4. Affine reserve, optimum slack, and paid continuation\n\nThe three ending-query affine reserves f_i-||g_i||^2/2 are\n\n    delta, u alpha+v beta-kappa, ell Z.             (11)\n\nThey are not the optimum slacks. Put t_star=min(Mv+beta,kappa/v).\nFor any sample of slope g_i and affine cost k_i the actual optimum\nslack is\n\n    <g_i,x_i-x_star>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2\n        =k_i-<g_i,x_star>.\n\nIt is respectively0, kappa-v t_star, ell t_star for p,q,r. All are\nnonnegative, but positive affine reserve does not mean positive optimum\nslack, and neither alone permits holding continuation.\n\nFor any appended list of nonnegative physical steps of total mass tau,\nthe SAME function keeps gradient r at every appended query exactly when\n\n    0<=tau<=Tcarry,\n    Tcarry=min{ Z/ell,\n                (ell Z-C)/ell^2,\n                [(ell+v)Z+u C+kappa]/[ell(ell+v)] }. (12)\n\nAll three numbers are nonnegative by (2). The proposed terminal proximal\npoint becomes(C,Z-tau ell). Its comparisons with zero, the old p-plane,\nand the old q-plane give the three bounds separately. Each comparison\ndecreases with tau; every earlier appended partial mass is smaller.\nExposedness of r proves necessity. Throughout this legal holding range,\n\n    V_nr(tau)=V_nr(0)-2tau ell^2/R_nr^2.            (13)\n\nThe minimizer set and initial distance do not change. No unchanged\ngradient is claimed after (12), and a changed-gradient continuation\nrequires new analysis of the actual function.\n\n## 5. Exact elimination of beta and unsaturated middle intercept\n\nFor every member of (1)--(2) change ONLY\n\n    beta'=0, kappa'=u alpha,                       (14)\n\nkeeping the same physical word, partition and u,v,ell,alpha. This is a\ncomparison of complete static instances; the function is not replaced\nmid-trajectory. The first two constraints in (2) imply\n\n    Mv^2<=(1+u)delta+u alpha.                      (15)\n\nThis is exactly the new first activity condition; the new middle\nreserve is0. At the final proximal point Z'=Z+beta, C is unchanged.\nThe new r-plane height rises by ell beta. The new q-plane height is\n\n    -u C-v Z'-u alpha=-E(u^2+v^2)+Nv ell.\n\nThe old q-plane height minus this value is u alpha+v beta-kappa>=0.\nThus all three final comparisons remain valid. This proves legality of\n(14) using every physical query, not a formal relaxation.\n\nThe original radius is at least the new radius because\n\n    Mv+beta-kappa/v>=Mv-u alpha/v.\n\nThe new terminal numerator is the old positive numerator plus2ell beta.\nTherefore (14) weakly increases the true-distance payoff. For an upper\nbound on the whole family it suffices to consider beta=0,kappa=u alpha:\n\n    delta=A-Mu-alpha>=0,\n    Mv^2<=(1+u)(A-Mu)-alpha,\n    R_0^2=(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2.           (16)\n\nIts final constraints still apply, but completing the square gives the\nvalid upper bound\n\n    V_nr<= E^2 v^2/(sigma R_0^2).                 (17)\n\nThe omitted final inequalities in (17) make it an upper relaxation,\nnever an asserted attainable objective. This distinction is essential\nin the next reduction.\n\n## 6. The nonradial scalar comparison, including both radius branches\n\nWe prove, under (4), for EVERY parameter in (16),\n\n    v^2/R_0^2<=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.    (18)\n\nIf M<=A, dropping alpha from (16) gives\n\n    v^2<=(1+u)(A-Mu)/M<=(A+M)^2/(4M^2)\n        <=A^2/M^2.\n\nThe middle inequality follows by completing the square in u; its\nvertex (A-M)/(2M) lies in[0,A/M]. Since R_0>=T, this proves the first\nalternative in (18), without any assumption on T in this regime.\n\nNow let M>=A. Put\n\n    P=A-Mu, k=1+u, c=uP, d=delta=P-alpha,\n    0<=d<=P, x=v^2, H=T+d, c_0=u alpha/M.\n\nFor fixed u,alpha, the ratio in (18) is\n\n    x/[H^2+M^2(x-c_0)_+^2/x].                     (19)\n\nOn x<=c_0 it is x/H^2 and increases. On x>=c_0 it is\n\n    x^2/[M^2x^2+(H^2-2M^2c_0)x+M^2c_0^2].\n\nThe derivative has the sign of\nx[(H^2-2M^2c_0)x+2M^2c_0^2]. This is nonnegative since\n\n    2M^2c_0=2M u alpha<=2M u(A-Mu)<=A^2/2<=T^2.\n\nThe two formulas agree at their boundary. Consequently (19) is largest\nat its permitted upper endpoint\n\n    x=(d+c)/M.\n\nAt that endpoint Mx-u alpha=(1+u)d>=0, so the resulting ratio is\n\n    F_u(d)=(d+c)^2 /\n            [M(d+c)(T+d)^2+M^2d^2 k^2].           (20)\n\nWe next show it is nondecreasing in d on[0,P]. At positive denominators\nits derivative has the sign of\n\n    S(d)=(d+c)(T+d)(T-d-2c)-2Mdc k^2.             (21)\n\nDirect differentiation gives S''(d)=-6(d+c)<=0. Thus its minimum over\nthe interval is at least the smaller endpoint value. At d=0,\n\n    S(0)=cT(T-2c)>=0,\n\nbecause c=u(A-Mu)<=A^2/(4M)<=A/4 and T>=sqrt(2)A. At d=P,\n\n    S(P)=P(1+u) D(u),\n    D(u)=(T+P)^2-2P(1+u)(T+A)\n        =M[M+2(T+A)]u^2-2A(T+A)u+T^2-A^2.\n\nCompleting the square and using M>=A yields\n\n    D(u)>=T^2-A^2-A^2(T+A)^2/[M(M+2(T+A))]\n         >=T^2-A^2-A(T+A)^2/(2T+3A)\n          =2(T+A)(T^2-2A^2)/(2T+3A)>=0.           (22)\n\nTherefore S(d)>=0 and (20) increases. The possible endpoint c=d=0\nis interpreted by its limit0; x>0 excludes it from the actual family.\nThe case P=0 has no legal v>0. All divisions made above are consequently\nlegitimate on the actual family, with continuity at relaxed boundaries.\n\nIt follows that (20) is at most its value at d=P, which is the scalar\nexpression obtained by setting alpha=0 and saturating (15). This is\nONLY a comparison of upper relaxations: it need not retain the final\ngradient's activity condition and is not a homogeneous conversion law.\n\nWriting t=P and C_0=M+A, that expression is\n\n    Psi(t)=t(C_0-t)/[M^2(T^2+(2T+C_0)t)], 0<=t<=A.\n\nIts derivative has the sign of\nC_0 T^2-2T^2t-(2T+C_0)t^2, with positive zero\nt_0=TC_0/(2T+C_0). If A>=t_0 the maximum is\nC_0^2/[M^2(2T+C_0)^2]<=A^2/(M^2T^2). If A<=t_0 the maximum is\n\n    A/[M((T+A)^2+MA)]<=1/(T+A+M)^2.\n\nFor the last inequality, the positive-denominator difference equals\n(T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0. Its sign is exactly the condition A<=t_0.\nThis proves (18), and (17) proves (5).\n\nThis is the new part of the joint proof. Independent alpha does buy true\nradius, but it cannot escape (18). The argument does not re-use the\nradial linkage alpha=zeta u,beta=zeta v or C102's word condition.\n\n## 7. Original ruler mass and every checkpoint pair\n\nFix the actual enclosing m>=128, abbreviate a=a_m,c_r=c_m,rho=c_r-2,\nand write a_k=a c_r^k for a level-k excess. The exact mass recurrence is\n\n    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+a c_r^k,\n    M_k=[a/(c_r-2)]c_r^k+[1-a/(c_r-2)]2^k.        (23)\n\nSince sqrt(2)<10/7,\n\n    12/5<lambda<c_r<49/20,\n    0<a<lambda-2<rho<=9/20,\n    M_k<=c_r^k, M_(k+1)<=c_r M_k,\n    a_k<=rho M_k.                                (24)\n\nFor the upper bound on c_r use\nc_r<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20. The remaining claims follow\nfrom a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda]<1/lambda=lambda-2 and (23).\n\nA level-k checkpoint has a local H_k immediately before and after it.\nIts complete prefix and suffix masses plus1 are both at least M_k.\nIn particular any first checkpoint in a pair has A/T<=rho, so\n2A^2/T^2<=2(9/20)^2<1, proving (4) for every pair.\n\nFor a pair i<j of levels k,l define, using its actual masses,\n\n    R_pair=AE/(TM), q_pair=c_r rho^2<1/2.          (25)\n\nWe prove R_pair<=q_pair for EVERY pair. If i is before the entire H_l\nimmediately preceding j, then T>=M_k and M>=M_l, giving the stronger\nbound rho^2. This case includes k>=l since such a level cannot lie\ninside H_l.\n\nOtherwise i lies in that local H_l, and k<l. Let x be1 plus its local\nprefix mass there and y=M. Both are at least M_k, and x+y=M_l-a_k.\nHence TM>=xy>=M_k(M_l-a_k-M_k). Write theta=a_k/M_k<=rho and\nz=M_l/M_k>=2+theta. Then\n\n    R_pair<=theta rho z/(z-1-theta)\n          <=theta rho(2+theta)<=rho^2(2+rho)=q_pair.\n\nHere z/(z-1-theta) decreases for z>=2+theta. Finally\nq_pair<=(49/20)(9/20)^2=3969/8000<1/2.\n\nPalindromicity applies the SAME pair estimate in reverse and yields\nAE/(NM)<=q_pair. The local side bounds also give A<=rho T,E<=rho N.\nConsequently\n\n    R_pair<=q_pair, T<=q_pair N/R_pair,\n    M<=rho^2 N/R_pair.\n\nThe exact total mass identity, with no lost ordinary queries, is\n\n    M_m=T+M+N+A+E.\n\nMultiply by R_pair, substitute the preceding three inequalities, and\nuse R_pair<=q_pair to get\n\n    R_pair<=min{q_pair,D_pair N/M_m},\n    D_pair=2(1+rho)q_pair+rho^2.                  (26)\n\nSince q_pair<1/2, rho<=9/20, and c_r>=12/5,\n\n    q_pair D_pair<=661/800,\n    D_pair/q_pair=2(1+rho)+1/c_r<=199/60<4.\n\nThe actual M_m exceeds8. The crossover N_0=q_pair M_m/D_pair thus\nexceeds1. On N<=N_0 use that N^2/(2N-1) increases for N>=1; on\nN>=N_0 use that1/(2N-1) decreases. Formula (26) gives\n\n    R_pair^2/(2N-1)<=q_pair D_pair/(2M_m-D_pair/q_pair),\n    [R_pair^2/(2N-1)]/[1/(2M_m-1)]\n       <=(661/800)(2M_m-1)/(2M_m-199/60)\n       <=5949/6088.                              (27)\n\nThe last rational function decreases with M_m; at M_m=8 it equals\n5949/6088. Every pair, its original tail and both ordering cases are\nincluded. This rederives the bound instead of importing the C102 draft.\n\n## 8. Complete same-word K, ray and lens comparisons\n\nWe first bound EVERY single-cut term of accepted b98bb807d514e80d.\nFor a fixed level-k step h=1+a_k and total mass S_total=M_m-1, its\ndenominator as a function of its complete prefix mass P is\n\n    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)].\n\nIts derivative has the sign of2(S_total-h)-3P. Its minimum between\nthe first and last occurrence is at an endpoint. The first occurrence\nhas prefix M_k-1 and suffix at least M_(m-1)-1; the last has those\nmasses reversed. In the last case use the increasing function\nx^2/(2x-1) for x>=1 and M_k<=M_(m-1). Both cases show that every cut\nis at most rho^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). Thus\n\n    Cut_i/[1/(2M_m-1)]<=rho^2(2c_r-1)\n        <=3159/4000<gamma103.                    (28)\n\nTo see the middle estimate, use M_m<=c_r M_(m-1) and\n(2c_r x-1)/(2x-1)<=2c_r-1 for x>=1. Therefore the COMPLETE K_n(H_m)\nis exactly its canonical Huber term1/(2M_m-1).\n\nFor the complete alternating-ray reference use precisely the recurrence\nand feasibility conditions in accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a. A selection with\nat least two checkpoints has first magnitudes m_0=1/T and m_1=A/(TM),\nfirst old offset (T-1)/T, and budget after the second checkpoint\n\n    E m_1-(T-1)m_0=m_0[R_pair T-(T-1)]<m_0,       (29)\n\nby R_pair<1. With exactly two checkpoints its feasibility requires this\nbudget at least N m_0>=m_0. With more checkpoints its next nonterminal\nmagnitude is no greater than this budget, contradicting the required\nsame-ray m_2>=m_0. Hence no such selection is feasible. The zero- and\nsingle-checkpoint choices are exactly the K terms. This proves\n\n    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1).             (30)\n\nFor a pair in (5), the first term is EXACTLY the second checkpoint's\nsingle-cut term: its prefix mass plus1 is T+A+M, and its suffix factor\nis2N-1. Apply (28) to that term and (27) to the second. This proves (6)\nuniformly over the entire declared nonradial family and all partitions.\n\nThe COMPLETE universal-lens reference from dfde2fa326c908bf is\nV_lens(h)=max{sup_(z in Lens_n)Gap_h(z),1/(1+2sum h)}. Original all-split B_n normalization\n\nThe accepted original recurrences in b98bb807d514e80d are, with\nC_n=1+S_circle(n), B_n=1+2S_bullet(n), C_0=B_0=1,\n\n    C_n=max_(k+l+1=n)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n)G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.\n\nWe rederive the stronger estimate needed here. Put p_0=log_2(lambda)>1\nand f_0(t)=(1+t)^p_0-1 for t>0. Its logarithm, as a function of log t,\nhas second derivative\n\n    p_0 t(1+t)^(p_0-2)\n       [(1+t)^p_0-1-p_0 t]/f_0(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive since it vanishes at0 and has positive\nderivative for t>0. Zero arguments follow by continuity.\nInduction over the ORIGINAL maximum of ALL splits proves C_n<=(n+1)^p_0.\nThe inequality G(a,d)<=4a+d then gives, by a second induction,\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^p_0.                               (31)\n\nFor each actual split k+l+1=n use (k+1)^p_0+l^p_0<=n^p_0; this follows\nby integrating the increasing derivative of x^p_0, and includes l=0.\nNo selected or reoptimized denominator sequence replaces the original.\n\nUsing (23) with its enclosing-m parameters gives\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n      =(1+1/m)^m [a_m/(c_m-2)\n          +(1-a_m/(c_m-2))(2/c_m)^m] ->exp(1).\n\nHere a_m/(c_m-2)->1 and2/c_m is uniformly below1. Since\n(2^m-1)^p_0/lambda^m->1, (6) and (31) give (7). The upper comparison\nsequence tends to2gamma103/exp(1); no convergence of the actual supremum\nis assumed. Its bound is strictly below1 since gamma103<1 and exp(1)>2.\n\n## 10. Scope and what is now closed\n\nThis joint draft closes the original-H_m payoff comparison of EVERY\nparameter and EVERY checkpoint pair in C102 Section13, including both\nnearest-minimizer branches, u=0, positive reserves, arbitrary allowed\nintercepts, alpha and beta independent, and arbitrary nonnegative\nordinary block contents. An actual acute endpoint-angle law outside the closed family\n\nHere is concrete static data for the next distinct hypothesis; it is\nnot merely a Gram matrix or a proposed optimization problem. It works on any actual two-checkpoint word as in Section1.\n\nKeep T,M,N,A,E as above and set\n\n    zeta=E/2, B=E/2, D=M+zeta,\n    v=sqrt(A/(4D)),\n    u=min{A/(4D), Bv^2/(8N), N/B},\n    alpha=zeta u, beta=zeta v, kappa=zeta(u^2+v^2)/2,\n    delta=A-Du,\n    z=(C,V)=B(u,v), r=(C/(4N),V/(2N)),\n    p=e_1, q=(-u,-v),\n    J_ang(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},\n    F_ang(x)=min_y{J_ang(y)+||x-y||^2/2},\n    X_0=(T+delta,-Dv). The final gradient has strictly\npositive first and second coordinates, so p and r form an ACUTE angle.\nThe middle and final gradients are also nonparallel: their component\nratios are v/u and2v/u. (34)\n\nFor the SAME function, holding continuation has EXACT total allowance\n\n    T_ang=N(3C^2+4V^2-12NC)/(C^2+4V^2-4NC),\n    0<T_ang<N. Consequently\nthe old FIRST plane is precisely the limiting constraint, while the\nother two comparisons remain strictly legal at mass T_ang. Every\nearlier appended partial mass is smaller. The gradient r is exposed\nby e_2, so it cannot remain the actual gradient once its p comparison\nfails. (36)\n\nOn every original H_m with m>=3, one explicit whole-word partition is\ni=1,j=n-2, the first and last level1 checkpoint. Original certificate and target region\n\nLet h=(u,w,z,v) be four nonnegative physical steps, and put\n\n s=u+w+z+v, C=1+s.\n\nThe original full primitive certificate has twenty nonnegative numbers\nLambda_ij for distinct i,j in{0,1,2,3,4}, with Lambda_ii=0. Every original coordinate is retained, including at zero\nsteps. There is no sparsity, rank, reversal, or symmetry restriction.\n\nFor any actual gradient-descent trajectory of a convex 1-smooth\nfunction, write X_i for the positions, G_i for the gradients, F_i for\nthe values, and define\n\n Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(F_i-F_4)\n       +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2-sC||G_4||^2.      (1)\n\nThe accepted identity from04219554e41d9750 is\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(G_0,...,G_4)>.  (2)\n\nHere the matrix inner product is trace of the transpose times the\nsecond matrix. Since every Q_ij<=0, an actual trajectory with V_h<0\nexcludes every such original certificate.\n\nThe region considered in this document is\n\n h>=0, C>=C0:=193/25, w<=4/3, 6w+7v<=18.             (3)\n\nThere is no fixed first step, no third-step interval or upper bound,\nand no assumed ordering of the four steps. In particular (3) contains\nthe entire threshold part of w<=4/3,v<=10/7, as well as the additional\nregion v>10/7,w<3-7v/6. The conclusion will be that (3) has no full\nprimitive certificate. It is not a reduction of all threshold words\nto this region.\n\n## 2. The cut conditions, with their actual functions\n\nFor an index j in{0,1,2,3} put Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i and Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i.\nEvery full primitive certificate necessarily satisfies\n\n C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).                         (4)\n\nTo prove (4) when h_j>1, set A=1+Pj,B=1+Rj,r=(h_j-1)/B>0.\nUse the single one-dimensional function\n\n F_cut(x)=max_(g in[-r,1]) (xg-g^2/2), X_0=A.\n\nIts derivative is the projection of x onto[-r,1]. Up to query j the\npositions are 1 plus the remaining prefix sum, so their derivative is1.\nThe selected update takes X_j=1 to-(h_j-1)=-Br. Every later position\nis -r times one plus its remaining tail sum, so the derivative is-r\nand X_4=-r. All sampled values equal X_i G_i-G_i^2/2.\n\nFor any saturated values F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2, expansion of the\nupdates gives\n\n V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_4+sG_4||^2.                     (5)\n\nIndeed 2sum h_i<X_i,G_i>-sum h_i^2||G_i||^2 telescopes to\n||X_0||^2-||X_4||^2; the remaining terms in(1) are\n-2s<X_4,G_4>-s^2||G_4||^2. Thus the cut function has\nV_h=A^2-C^2r^2, proving(4) by(2). When h_j<=1, (4) is automatic.\nThis construction includes zero steps elsewhere in the word. It has\nan actual minimizer0 and is convex and1-smooth by the projection\nargument given explicitly in the next section.\n\nIn particular the first and last cuts imply\n\n u<=2C/(C+1)<2, v<=2C/(C+1)<2.                      (6)\n\nFor a step at most1 these bounds are automatic, since C>=193/25>1.\nFor a step exceeding1 they follow by rearranging C(u-1)<=C-u, or\nrespectively C(v-1)<=C-v.\n\n## 3. Complete phase domain of one nested planar family\n\nFor this section only, assume\n\n u>1, w>=0, z>1, v>=0,\n a=u-1, m=1+w, c=z-1, D=1+v,\n N=u+m, q=a/m>0, t=c/D>0,\n C=N+D+c=N+D(1+t).                                  (7)\n\nNo upper bound on u,w,z,v is needed here. Given an amplitude rho>=0,\ndefine five base and auxiliary scalar positions and gradients by\n\n Xbase=(N,m,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),\n Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq),\n X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Xaux_i),\n G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)*Gaux_i),\n F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                          (8)\n\nThese are the original four physical updates. The base updates use\nN-u=m,m-w=1,1-z=-c,-c+vt=-t. The auxiliary updates use\n1-u=-a,-a+wq=-q,-q+zq=cq,cq-vtq=tq.\n\nFor the saturated values in(8), the residual is exactly\n\n Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>.                            (9)\n\nThe base target normals are(N-1,w,0,-vt,0); the auxiliary target\nnormals are(0,-wq,0,vtq,0). Here is the complete finite-row check,\norganized by the target index j; omitted diagonal terms are zero.\n\n * Target0: sources1,2 give0; sources3,4 give-(1+t)(N-1).\n * Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q); source2 gives0;\n   sources3,4 give-w*(1+t)*(1+rho*q^2).\n * Targets2 and4: every source gives0.\n * Target3: sources1,2 give-vt*(1+t)*(1+rho*q^2);\n   source4 gives0; source0 gives\n\n       Q_03=vt[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-tq)].                 (10)\n\nAll twenty directed finite rows are therefore nonpositive exactly when\n\n 0<=rho<=(1+t)/(q(1-tq))  if v>0 and tq<1;\n arbitrary rho>=0        if v=0 or tq>=1.             (11)\n\nThe equality cases v=0 and tq=1 are included. Necessity of this phase\ncondition is the single row(10), and sufficiency is the full list above.\n\nAdd the optimum jet X_star=G_star=F_star=0. All Q_(i,star) vanish.\nFor Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,X_i-G_i>, the respective inner products are\n\n N-1, w*(1+rho*q^2), 0, vt^2*(1+rho*q^2), 0,\n\nwhich are all nonnegative. Thus every optimum row holds as well.\n\nAn explicit single static function is\n\n K_rho=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3},\n F_rho(X)=max_(G in K_rho)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).         (12)\n\nThe other sampled gradients satisfy G_2=G_1 and G_4=G_3=-tG_1, so\n0 belongs to K_rho. Equation(9), for every source vertex, is precisely\nthe condition <G-G_i,X_i-G_i><=0 for all G in K_rho. Completing the\nsquare identifies the maximizer in(12) with the unique Euclidean\nprojection of X onto K_rho. Consequently it is G_i at X_i, and(12)\nhas the values F_i in(8).\n\nTo verify smoothness directly, the two projection inequalities at\npoints X,X' give <X-X',P(X)-P(X')>>=||P(X)-P(X')||^2, where P is\nprojection onto K_rho. Hence P is1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing\ninequalities give\n\n 0<=F_rho(X')-F_rho(X)-<P(X),X'-X>\n     <=<P(X')-P(X),X'-X><=||X'-X||^2.\n\nThus F_rho is differentiable with gradient P, and is convex as a\nmaximum of affine functions of X. It is finite since K_rho is compact.\nFinally 0 in K_rho implies F_rho>=0=F_rho(0). Its true minimizer set\nis nonempty; no assertion of uniqueness or normalized payoff is needed.\n\nBy(5) and X_4=G_4, the exact primitive energy is\n\n V_h(rho)=N^2-C^2t^2+rho*(1-C^2t^2q^2).             (13)\n\nThis proves the complete all-amplitude test within the explicit family.\nIf v>0 and tq<1, it is equivalent to testing rho=0 and the upper\nendpoint in(11), since(13) is affine in rho. If v=0 or tq>=1, it is\nequivalent to both N^2-C^2t^2>=0 and1-C^2t^2q^2>=0. In particular\ntq>=1 is impossible for a full certificate on(7), because C>1.\nThese are statements only about the family(8),(12); no classification\nof all legal planar functions or all scalar trajectories is asserted.\n\n## 4. Exact reduced energy in the relevant phase\n\nSuppose0<tq<1 and choose the legal amplitude\n\n rho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq)).\n\nIt remains legal when v=0, even though that case also allows larger\namplitudes. Define\n\n k=(q*N^2-1/q)/(1+q)\n   =u*(N-m/(u-1))\n   =u^2+m*u-m-m/(u-1),\n Gred(u,m,C,D)=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2*t^2,\n N=u+m, t=(C-N-D)/D.                                (14)\n\nTo check the formula for k, factor its numerator using\n(u-1)N+m=u(N-1) and m+u-1=N-1. Direct expansion of(13) then gives\n\n V_h(rho)=(1+q)/(1-tq)*Gred(u,m,C,D).                (15)\n\nThe factor is positive. No feasibility of a formal covariance matrix\nor deletion of an interpolation pair has entered this reduction.\n\n## 5. All old-cut survivors in(3) enter this phase\n\nAssume all cuts(4) hold for a word in(3). Then(6) gives u,v<2, and\n\n z=C-1-u-w-v > C0-19/3=104/75>1.                    (16)\n\nSet m=1+w,D=1+v,N=u+m. These have1<=m<=7/3 and1<=D<3. The third\ncut gives Ct<=N, or equivalently\n\n N>=fcut(C,D):=C(C-D)/(C+D).                         (17)\n\nHere C>D. The function fcut increases in C and decreases in D,\nas follows from\n\n partial_C fcut=1-2D^2/(C+D)^2>0,\n partial_D fcut=-2C^2/(C+D)^2<0.\n\nPut D0=17/7. If D<=D0, then\n\n N>=fcut(C0,D0)=89359/22200>4,\n u=N-m>5/3.                                         (18)\n\nIf D>=D0, the last condition in(3) becomes\n\n m<=mline(D):=(31-7D)/6.\n\nOn D0<=D<3 we have5/3<mline(D)<=7/3. Furthermore\nfcut(C0,D)-mline(D) is strictly increasing in D, since\n\n C0/(C0+D)<=1351/1776<16/21,\n d/dD [fcut(C0,D)-mline(D)]\n      >=7/6-512/441=5/882>0.\n\nIts value at D0 is greater than5/3 by(18). Thus in both cases\n\n 5/3<u<2, 0<q=(u-1)/m<1, 0<t<=N/C<1.               (19)\n\nThis proves the required physical phase, including all potential zero\nand small values of w or v. It also proves u>1 rather than assuming\naway a first step at most1.\n\n## 6. Reduction of the whole region to one rational corner\n\nFor5/3<u<2 and1<=m<=7/3, the coefficient k in(14) is positive.\nIndeed its derivatives in m and u are\n\n k_m=u-1-1/(u-1)<0,\n k_u=2u+m+m/(u-1)^2>0,\n\nso its minimum on the closed bounding rectangle is\nk(5/3,7/3)=5/6>0. Wherever t>0, differentiation of(14) gives\n\n partial_C Gred=-k/D-2C*t^2-2C^2*t/D<0,\n partial_D Gred=(k+2C^2*t)*(C-N)/D^2>0,\n partial_m Gred=2N-k_m*(1+t)+k/D+2C^2*t/D>0.         (20)\n\nLet Dhat=max(D,D0), and mhat=mline(Dhat). Conditions(3) imply\nm<=mhat, D<=Dhat. The first cut also gives\n\n C>=Cmin(u):=max(C0,u/(2-u)).\n\nIncreasing m and D to mhat and Dhat, and decreasing C to Cmin, in\n(20) therefore gives\n\n Gred(u,m,C,D)<=Gred(u,mline(Dhat),Cmin(u),Dhat).       (21)\n\nAll intermediate arguments of(20) have t>0. For example at the\nsmallest C, largest u and the final m,D one has\n\n t=(C0-u-31/6)/Dhat+1/6>79/225>0,\n\nusing u<2 and Dhat<3; earlier comparison points have larger C-N-D.\nThe coefficient k stays positive by the rectangle bound above.\nThe virtual comparison parameters need not satisfy every old cut;\nonly the proven sign conditions for(20) are used.\n\nFor each fixed Dhat in[D0,3], write m=mline(Dhat). The function on\nthe right of(21) has its maximum in5/3<u<2 at\n\n U0=2C0/(C0+1)=193/109.                              (22)\n\nHere is a full derivative proof, retaining both sides of the first-cut\nboundary. If5/3<u<=U0, then Cmin=C0. Since U0<9/5, the formulas give\n\n N>10/3, 2/5<t<3/5, k_u<12, C0>7, Dhat<=3.\n\nFor the lower t bound, C0-u-31/6>113/150 and thus\nt>113/450+1/6=94/225>2/5. (23)\n\nIf U0<=u<2, then Cmin=C_u:=u/(2-u). Since U0>7/4,\n\n C_u'=2/(2-u)^2>32,\n t_u=(C_u'-1)/Dhat>1.\n\nAt U0 the value of t exceeds2/5, and it increases thereafter.\nNow k,k_u are positive, C_u>7 and N<13/3. The derivative is\n\n 2N-k_u*(1+t)-k*t_u-2C_u*C_u'*t^2-2C_u^2*t*t_u\n   <26/3-98/3=-24<0.                               (24)\n\nEquations(23),(24) establish(22), including equality at the boundary.\n\n## 7. The remaining tail comparison, entirely analytically\n\nFix u=U0,C=C0 and let D range in[D0,3], with m=mline(D). Put\n\n Astar=C0-U0-31/6=12797/16350, t=Astar/D+1/6,\n Hred(D)=Gred(U0,mline(D),C0,D).\n\nExact rational comparisons give0<Astar<4/5, U0>7/4,\nU0^2<16/5 and C0^2<60. Differentiating, with k_m evaluated at U0,\ngives\n\n Hred'(D)=-7N/3+(7/6)*k_m*(1+t)\n                    +Astar*(k+2C0^2*t)/D^2.         (25)\n\nSince k_m<0 and k<U0^2<16/5, while\nN>(83-14D)/12, (25) is strictly less than -Fbound(D), where\n\n Fbound(D)=(581-98D)/36-464/(25D^2)-384/(5D^3).       (26)\n\nFor the positive term in(25), the stated estimate follows from\n\n (4/5)/D^2 *[16/5+120*(4/(5D)+1/6)]\n       =464/(25D^2)+384/(5D^3).\n\nThe derivative of(26) is\n\n Fbound'(D)=-49/18+928/(25D^3)+1152/(5D^4)>0,\n\nbecause D<=3 gives1152/(5D^4)>=128/45>49/18.\nAlso12/5<D0<5/2, so\n\n Fbound(D0)>28/3-29/9-50/9=5/9.\n\nTherefore Hred'(D)<-5/9 on the entire closed interval, and its\nmaximum occurs at D0. This includes the diagonal extension of the\noriginal w<=4/3,v<=10/7 slab; changing v up to the endpoint2 has not\nbeen justified by an unsupported extrapolation of a fixed trajectory.\n\nAt the one remaining rational corner, the exact quantities are\n\n u=193/109, m=7/3, D=17/7, C=193/25,\n N=1342/327<411/100,\n t=67952/138975, 1+t>37/25,\n k=815039/427716>19/10,\n Ct=13114736/3474375>377/100.\n\nConsequently\n\n Gred < (411/100)^2-(19/10)*(37/25)-(377/100)^2\n      =-83/625.                                     (27)\n\nAll quantities bounded below in the product k(1+t) are positive.\nCombining(21)--(27) proves this same strict bound for every original\nword in(3) satisfying all cuts(4).\n\n## 8. Main regional conclusion and remaining scope\n\nFor every nonnegative four-step word h=(u,w,z,v) satisfying\n\n sum h>=168/25, w<=4/3, 6w+7v<=18,\n\nthere is no original twenty-nonnegative-multiplier, exact-flow,\nfull-R5 positive semidefinite primitive certificate.\n\nIndeed, if any cut(4) fails, the explicit one-dimensional function of\nSection2 already has V_h<0. Multiplying F by any L>0 preserves (3) under\nthe original steps h_i/L.\n\nThe new general word-conditioned bound is: if\n\n    T>=sqrt(2) A,                                  (4)\n\nthen EVERY member of (1)--(2) satisfies\n\n    V_nr <= max{ E^2/[sigma(T+A+M)^2],\n                 (AE/(TM))^2/sigma }.             (5)\n\nThe first term is the accepted single-cut witness at the SECOND actual\ncheckpoint. The second term retains the actual first, middle and tail\nblock masses. No parameter optimum is left undecided in the comparison.\nAn intermediate scalar relaxation below is not asserted to be attained\nby a legal homogeneous instance.\n\nFor the ORIGINAL enclosing-m ruler use, throughout every child word,\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_m=1-1/m,\n    c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,\n    H_0=(), H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a_m c_m^k,H_k),\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, M_k=1+sum H_k.\n\nThe local beta in (1) is NOT beta_m. For every m>=128, every pair of\nactual checkpoint indices satisfies (4), and\n\n    sup_(all parameters (2), all pairs on H_m) V_nr\n       <= gamma103/(2M_m-1)\n        = gamma103 K_n(H_m)\n        = gamma103 F_ray_n(H_m),\n    gamma103=61473/68840<1,                         (6)\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V_nr\n       <=2gamma103/exp(1)<1.                       (7)\n\nThus even the ENTIRE independent nonradial-middle family is weaker than\nthe complete same-word Huber/ray reference. The Huber-augmented complete\napplicable lens reference also dominates it. This is not an upper bound\non unrestricted W_n(H_m). A distinct endpoint-angle construction is\ngiven in Section12; Section14 also closes that particular safe parameter\nlaw. General endpoint angles remain outside both closures.\n\n## 2. The one static function, actual minimum and radius\n\nJ is a finite continuous convex nonnegative maximum of affine functions.\nFor fixed x its defining proximal objective is coercive and strictly\nconvex, so it has a unique minimizer y(x). Its optimality condition is\n\n    g(x):=x-y(x) in partial J(y(x)).                (8)\n\nFor completeness, a minimizer compared to y+t(z-y), convexity of J,\nand division by t>0 followed by t down to0 give the subgradient\ninequality J(z)>=J(y)+<x-y,z-y>. Conversely that inequality and the\nquadratic expansion imply that the objective at z exceeds its value\nat y by at least ||z-y||^2/2, proving (8).\n\nAdd the two subgradient inequalities at x,x'. With g=g(x),g'=g(x'),\none obtains <g-g',y-y'>>=0 and hence\n\n    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.\n\nThus g is1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the proximal objective for x' at y\ngives the upper bound in\n\n    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<g,x'-x><=||x'-x||^2/2.\n\nFor the lower bound, apply the subgradient inequality at y to y' and\nexpand the two quadratic terms; the remainder is ||g'-g||^2/2.\nThese inequalities prove differentiability with gradient g and convexity\nby the supporting affine function at each x. Therefore F is everywhere\ndefined, convex and1-smooth.\n\nIts minimum is0 and its minimizer set is exactly\n\n    K={x_1<=0,x_2<=0,-u x_1-v x_2<=kappa}.         (9)\n\nIndeed J is zero precisely there, and F(x)=0 forces both nonnegative\nsummands at the attained minimum to vanish. For u>0, K is the triangle\nwith vertices(0,0),(-kappa/u,0),(0,-kappa/v), allowing degeneracy.\nFor u=0 it is the strip x_1<=0,-kappa/v<=x_2<=0.\n\nEvery point of K has x_1<=0 and -kappa/v<=x_2<=0. The stated x_star\nlies in K and separately minimizes the two coordinate-square distances\nfrom X_0, whose first coordinate is positive and second is negative.\nIt is therefore the true unique closest minimizer. This proves both\npositive-part branches in (3); no old uncentered norm is substituted.\n\n## 3. All physical gradients and all original directed rows\n\nGive the four slopes p,q,r,0 the affine costs0,kappa,0,0. If slope g_i\nis active at y_i=x_i-g_i, then (8) gives its actual gradient and\n\n    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i.\n\nFor every ordered pair of such samples the residual from accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290 expands to\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>-k_i+k_j<=0.                 (10)\n\nThe last inequality is exactly activity of j's plane against i's at\ny_j. It proves BOTH orientations of every remote row, not merely\nadjacent or checkpoint rows. Include the optimum sample with position\nx_star and g_star=f_star=k_star=0, so y_star=x_star: the zero plane is\nactive there and the same argument proves both optimum rows.\n\nAt a physical prefix cumulative mass t in[0,S_0], the point and its\nproposed proximal point are\n\n    x=(T+delta-t,-Mv-beta),\n    y=(delta+S_0-t,-Mv-beta).\n\nAt the last prefix query p's plane dominates zero and q's plane exactly\nby the first two inequalities in (2); r's plane is negative. Earlier\nqueries add a nonnegative multiple of p, improving all comparisons\nsince p's directional slope1 exceeds the others0,-u,0.\n\nThe first checkpoint sends the point to(-Mu-alpha,-Mv-beta). At middle\ncumulative mass t in[0,S_1],\n\n    x=(-(M-t)u-alpha,-(M-t)v-beta),\n    y=(-alpha-(S_1-t)u,-beta-(S_1-t)v).\n\nAt t=S_1 the q-plane height is u alpha+v beta-kappa>=0, while p,r\nhave heights-alpha,-ell beta. Earlier queries add a nonnegative multiple\nof q; its directional slope u^2+v^2 exceeds0,-u,-ell v. Thus every\nmiddle query is legal.\n\nThe second checkpoint sends the point to(C,Ev-beta). At tail cumulative\nmass t in[0,S_2], its proposed proximal point is\n\n    y=(C,Ev-beta-(1+t)ell).\n\nAt the actual terminal query t=S_2, (2) compares r's plane against all\nthree competitors. Earlier tail queries add a nonnegative multiple of\ne_2, improving every comparison. This proves the complete physical\ntrajectory and (10), including large ordinary steps and zero steps.\n\nNecessity also holds: p,q,r are exposed vertices of the slope hull,\nexposed respectively by e_1,-e_2,e_2. A subgradient of a finite affine\nmaximum is a convex combination of its active slopes: if it were not,\na separating direction would contradict the directional derivative,\nwhich is the largest active directional slope. Thus an exposed slope\nas the actual gradient in (8) requires its own plane to be active.\nThe three ending-query comparisons give precisely (2).\n\nAt the terminal query the active-plane value formula gives\nf_n=ell(Ev-beta)-(S_2+1/2)ell^2, proving (3). It is positive because\nZ>=0 implies Ev-beta>=Nell and hence its numerator is at least ell^2.\n\n## 4. Affine reserve, optimum slack, and paid continuation\n\nThe three ending-query affine reserves f_i-||g_i||^2/2 are\n\n    delta, u alpha+v beta-kappa, ell Z.             (11)\n\nThey are not the optimum slacks. Put t_star=min(Mv+beta,kappa/v).\nFor any sample of slope g_i and affine cost k_i the actual optimum\nslack is\n\n    <g_i,x_i-x_star>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2\n        =k_i-<g_i,x_star>.\n\nIt is respectively0, kappa-v t_star, ell t_star for p,q,r. All are\nnonnegative, but positive affine reserve does not mean positive optimum\nslack, and neither alone permits holding continuation.\n\nFor any appended list of nonnegative physical steps of total mass tau,\nthe SAME function keeps gradient r at every appended query exactly when\n\n    0<=tau<=Tcarry,\n    Tcarry=min{ Z/ell,\n                (ell Z-C)/ell^2,\n                [(ell+v)Z+u C+kappa]/[ell(ell+v)] }. (12)\n\nAll three numbers are nonnegative by (2). The proposed terminal proximal\npoint becomes(C,Z-tau ell). Its comparisons with zero, the old p-plane,\nand the old q-plane give the three bounds separately. Each comparison\ndecreases with tau; every earlier appended partial mass is smaller.\nExposedness of r proves necessity. Throughout this legal holding range,\n\n    V_nr(tau)=V_nr(0)-2tau ell^2/R_nr^2.            (13)\n\nThe minimizer set and initial distance do not change. No unchanged\ngradient is claimed after (12), and a changed-gradient continuation\nrequires new analysis of the actual function.\n\n## 5. Exact elimination of beta and unsaturated middle intercept\n\nFor every member of (1)--(2) change ONLY\n\n    beta'=0, kappa'=u alpha,                       (14)\n\nkeeping the same physical word, partition and u,v,ell,alpha. This is a\ncomparison of complete static instances; the function is not replaced\nmid-trajectory. The first two constraints in (2) imply\n\n    Mv^2<=(1+u)delta+u alpha.                      (15)\n\nThis is exactly the new first activity condition; the new middle\nreserve is0. At the final proximal point Z'=Z+beta, C is unchanged.\nThe new r-plane height rises by ell beta. The new q-plane height is\n\n    -u C-v Z'-u alpha=-E(u^2+v^2)+Nv ell.\n\nThe old q-plane height minus this value is u alpha+v beta-kappa>=0.\nThus all three final comparisons remain valid. This proves legality of\n(14) using every physical query, not a formal relaxation.\n\nThe original radius is at least the new radius because\n\n    Mv+beta-kappa/v>=Mv-u alpha/v.\n\nThe new terminal numerator is the old positive numerator plus2ell beta.\nTherefore (14) weakly increases the true-distance payoff. For an upper\nbound on the whole family it suffices to consider beta=0,kappa=u alpha:\n\n    delta=A-Mu-alpha>=0,\n    Mv^2<=(1+u)(A-Mu)-alpha,\n    R_0^2=(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2.           (16)\n\nIts final constraints still apply, but completing the square gives the\nvalid upper bound\n\n    V_nr<= E^2 v^2/(sigma R_0^2).                 (17)\n\nThe omitted final inequalities in (17) make it an upper relaxation,\nnever an asserted attainable objective. This distinction is essential\nin the next reduction.\n\n## 6. The nonradial scalar comparison, including both radius branches\n\nWe prove, under (4), for EVERY parameter in (16),\n\n    v^2/R_0^2<=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.    (18)\n\nIf M<=A, dropping alpha from (16) gives\n\n    v^2<=(1+u)(A-Mu)/M<=(A+M)^2/(4M^2)\n        <=A^2/M^2.\n\nThe middle inequality follows by completing the square in u; its\nvertex (A-M)/(2M) lies in[0,A/M]. Since R_0>=T, this proves the first\nalternative in (18), without any assumption on T in this regime.\n\nNow let M>=A. Put\n\n    P=A-Mu, k=1+u, c=uP, d=delta=P-alpha,\n    0<=d<=P, x=v^2, H=T+d, c_0=u alpha/M.\n\nFor fixed u,alpha, the ratio in (18) is\n\n    x/[H^2+M^2(x-c_0)_+^2/x].                     (19)\n\nOn x<=c_0 it is x/H^2 and increases. On x>=c_0 it is\n\n    x^2/[M^2x^2+(H^2-2M^2c_0)x+M^2c_0^2].\n\nThe derivative has the sign of\nx[(H^2-2M^2c_0)x+2M^2c_0^2]. This is nonnegative since\n\n    2M^2c_0=2M u alpha<=2M u(A-Mu)<=A^2/2<=T^2.\n\nThe two formulas agree at their boundary. Consequently (19) is largest\nat its permitted upper endpoint\n\n    x=(d+c)/M.\n\nAt that endpoint Mx-u alpha=(1+u)d>=0, so the resulting ratio is\n\n    F_u(d)=(d+c)^2 /\n            [M(d+c)(T+d)^2+M^2d^2 k^2].           (20)\n\nWe next show it is nondecreasing in d on[0,P]. At positive denominators\nits derivative has the sign of\n\n    S(d)=(d+c)(T+d)(T-d-2c)-2Mdc k^2.             (21)\n\nDirect differentiation gives S''(d)=-6(d+c)<=0. Thus its minimum over\nthe interval is at least the smaller endpoint value. At d=0,\n\n    S(0)=cT(T-2c)>=0,\n\nbecause c=u(A-Mu)<=A^2/(4M)<=A/4 and T>=sqrt(2)A. At d=P,\n\n    S(P)=P(1+u) D(u),\n    D(u)=(T+P)^2-2P(1+u)(T+A)\n        =M[M+2(T+A)]u^2-2A(T+A)u+T^2-A^2.\n\nCompleting the square and using M>=A yields\n\n    D(u)>=T^2-A^2-A^2(T+A)^2/[M(M+2(T+A))]\n         >=T^2-A^2-A(T+A)^2/(2T+3A)\n          =2(T+A)(T^2-2A^2)/(2T+3A)>=0.           (22)\n\nTherefore S(d)>=0 and (20) increases. The possible endpoint c=d=0\nis interpreted by its limit0; x>0 excludes it from the actual family.\nThe case P=0 has no legal v>0. All divisions made above are consequently\nlegitimate on the actual family, with continuity at relaxed boundaries.\n\nIt follows that (20) is at most its value at d=P, which is the scalar\nexpression obtained by setting alpha=0 and saturating (15). This is\nONLY a comparison of upper relaxations: it need not retain the final\ngradient's activity condition and is not a homogeneous conversion law.\n\nWriting t=P and C_0=M+A, that expression is\n\n    Psi(t)=t(C_0-t)/[M^2(T^2+(2T+C_0)t)], 0<=t<=A.\n\nIts derivative has the sign of\nC_0 T^2-2T^2t-(2T+C_0)t^2, with positive zero\nt_0=TC_0/(2T+C_0). If A>=t_0 the maximum is\nC_0^2/[M^2(2T+C_0)^2]<=A^2/(M^2T^2). If A<=t_0 the maximum is\n\n    A/[M((T+A)^2+MA)]<=1/(T+A+M)^2.\n\nFor the last inequality, the positive-denominator difference equals\n(T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0. Its sign is exactly the condition A<=t_0.\nThis proves (18), and (17) proves (5).\n\nThis is the new part of the joint proof. Independent alpha does buy true\nradius, but it cannot escape (18). The argument does not re-use the\nradial linkage alpha=zeta u,beta=zeta v or C102's word condition.\n\n## 7. Original ruler mass and every checkpoint pair\n\nFix the actual enclosing m>=128, abbreviate a=a_m,c_r=c_m,rho=c_r-2,\nand write a_k=a c_r^k for a level-k excess. The exact mass recurrence is\n\n    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+a c_r^k,\n    M_k=[a/(c_r-2)]c_r^k+[1-a/(c_r-2)]2^k.        (23)\n\nSince sqrt(2)<10/7,\n\n    12/5<lambda<c_r<49/20,\n    0<a<lambda-2<rho<=9/20,\n    M_k<=c_r^k, M_(k+1)<=c_r M_k,\n    a_k<=rho M_k.                                (24)\n\nFor the upper bound on c_r use\nc_r<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20. The remaining claims follow\nfrom a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda]<1/lambda=lambda-2 and (23).\n\nA level-k checkpoint has a local H_k immediately before and after it.\nIts complete prefix and suffix masses plus1 are both at least M_k.\nIn particular any first checkpoint in a pair has A/T<=rho, so\n2A^2/T^2<=2(9/20)^2<1, proving (4) for every pair.\n\nFor a pair i<j of levels k,l define, using its actual masses,\n\n    R_pair=AE/(TM), q_pair=c_r rho^2<1/2.          (25)\n\nWe prove R_pair<=q_pair for EVERY pair. If i is before the entire H_l\nimmediately preceding j, then T>=M_k and M>=M_l, giving the stronger\nbound rho^2. This case includes k>=l since such a level cannot lie\ninside H_l.\n\nOtherwise i lies in that local H_l, and k<l. Let x be1 plus its local\nprefix mass there and y=M. Both are at least M_k, and x+y=M_l-a_k.\nHence TM>=xy>=M_k(M_l-a_k-M_k). Write theta=a_k/M_k<=rho and\nz=M_l/M_k>=2+theta. Then\n\n    R_pair<=theta rho z/(z-1-theta)\n          <=theta rho(2+theta)<=rho^2(2+rho)=q_pair.\n\nHere z/(z-1-theta) decreases for z>=2+theta. Finally\nq_pair<=(49/20)(9/20)^2=3969/8000<1/2.\n\nPalindromicity applies the SAME pair estimate in reverse and yields\nAE/(NM)<=q_pair. The local side bounds also give A<=rho T,E<=rho N.\nConsequently\n\n    R_pair<=q_pair, T<=q_pair N/R_pair,\n    M<=rho^2 N/R_pair.\n\nThe exact total mass identity, with no lost ordinary queries, is\n\n    M_m=T+M+N+A+E.\n\nMultiply by R_pair, substitute the preceding three inequalities, and\nuse R_pair<=q_pair to get\n\n    R_pair<=min{q_pair,D_pair N/M_m},\n    D_pair=2(1+rho)q_pair+rho^2.                  (26)\n\nSince q_pair<1/2, rho<=9/20, and c_r>=12/5,\n\n    q_pair D_pair<=661/800,\n    D_pair/q_pair=2(1+rho)+1/c_r<=199/60<4.\n\nThe actual M_m is at least16. The crossover N_0=q_pair M_m/D_pair thus\nexceeds1. On N<=N_0 use that N^2/(2N-1) increases for N>=1; on\nN>=N_0 use that1/(2N-1) decreases. Formula (26) gives\n\n    R_pair^2/(2N-1)<=q_pair D_pair/(2M_m-D_pair/q_pair),\n    [R_pair^2/(2N-1)]/[1/(2M_m-1)]\n       <=(661/800)(2M_m-1)/(2M_m-199/60)\n       <=61473/68840.                            (27)\n\nThe last rational function decreases with M_m; at M_m=16 it equals\n(661/800)*31/(32-199/60)=61473/68840. Every pair, its original tail\nand both ordering cases are\nincluded. This rederives the bound instead of importing the C102 draft.\n\n## 8. Complete same-word K, ray and lens comparisons\n\nWe first bound EVERY single-cut term of accepted b98bb807d514e80d.\nFor a fixed level-k step h=1+a_k and total mass S_total=M_m-1, its\ndenominator as a function of its complete prefix mass P is\n\n    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)].\n\nIts derivative has the sign of2(S_total-h)-3P. Its minimum between\nthe first and last occurrence is at an endpoint. The first occurrence\nhas prefix M_k-1 and suffix at least M_(m-1)-1; the last has those\nmasses reversed. In the last case use the increasing function\nx^2/(2x-1) for x>=1 and M_k<=M_(m-1). Both cases show that every cut\nis at most rho^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). Thus\n\n    Cut_i/[1/(2M_m-1)]<=rho^2(2c_r-1)\n        <=3159/4000<gamma103.                    (28)\n\nTo see the middle estimate, use M_m<=c_r M_(m-1) and\n(2c_r x-1)/(2x-1)<=2c_r-1 for x>=1. Therefore the COMPLETE K_n(H_m)\nis exactly its canonical Huber term1/(2M_m-1).\n\nFor the complete alternating-ray reference use precisely the recurrence\nand feasibility conditions in accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a. A selection with\nat least two checkpoints has first magnitudes m_0=1/T and m_1=A/(TM),\nfirst old offset (T-1)/T, and budget after the second checkpoint\n\n    E m_1-(T-1)m_0=m_0[R_pair T-(T-1)]<m_0,       (29)\n\nby R_pair<1. With exactly two checkpoints its feasibility requires this\nbudget at least N m_0>=m_0. With more checkpoints its next nonterminal\nmagnitude is no greater than this budget, contradicting the required\nsame-ray m_2>=m_0. Hence no such selection is feasible. The zero- and\nsingle-checkpoint choices are exactly the K terms. This proves\n\n    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1).             (30)\n\nFor a pair in (5), the first term is EXACTLY the second checkpoint's\nsingle-cut term: its prefix mass plus1 is T+A+M, and its suffix factor\nis2N-1. Apply (28) to that term and (27) to the second. This proves (6)\nuniformly over the entire declared nonradial family and all partitions.\n\nThe COMPLETE universal-lens reference from dfde2fa326c908bf is\nV_lens(h)=max{sup_(z in Lens_n)Gap_h(z),1/(1+2sum h)}. Its actual\ndefinition contains the same Huber term. Therefore\n\n    V_nr<=gamma103 V_lens(H_m).\n\nThis compares the complete applicable reference, not sampled complex\nparameters. It neither computes its complex component nor claims that\nthe larger schedule-dependent domain D_h equals Lens_n. No upper bound\non all nonlinear scalar functions is implied.\n\nC59/e9a55f57461f253c is retained ONLY for its actual enclosing-H6 word\nwith epsilon1/6. The present m>=128 theorem neither transfers that\nwitness to altered-parameter subwords nor asserts a new H6 record.\nAccepted62a7d9329121fd75 remains the finite(2,2,1) calibration only;\nit is not a premise for iteration or for this family comparison.\n\n## 9. Original all-split B_n normalization\n\nThe accepted original recurrences in b98bb807d514e80d are, with\nC_n=1+S_circle(n), B_n=1+2S_bullet(n), C_0=B_0=1,\n\n    C_n=max_(k+l+1=n)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n)G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.\n\nWe rederive the stronger estimate needed here. Put p_0=log_2(lambda)>1\nand f_0(t)=(1+t)^p_0-1 for t>0. Its logarithm, as a function of log t,\nhas second derivative\n\n    p_0 t(1+t)^(p_0-2)\n       [(1+t)^p_0-1-p_0 t]/f_0(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive since it vanishes at0 and has positive\nderivative for t>0. Convexity at log t,-log t gives\nf_0(t)f_0(1/t)>=f_0(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. It follows that\n\n    Omega(a,d)<=(a^(1/p_0)+d^(1/p_0))^p_0.\n\nIndeed the right side X satisfies (X-a)(X-d)>=2ad, the positive-root\nequation for Omega, and X>=max(a,d). Zero arguments follow by continuity.\nInduction over the ORIGINAL maximum of ALL splits proves C_n<=(n+1)^p_0.\nThe inequality G(a,d)<=4a+d then gives, by a second induction,\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^p_0.                               (31)\n\nFor each actual split k+l+1=n use (k+1)^p_0+l^p_0<=n^p_0; this follows\nby integrating the increasing derivative of x^p_0, and includes l=0.\nNo selected or reoptimized denominator sequence replaces the original.\n\nUsing (23) with its enclosing-m parameters gives\n\n    M_m/lambda^m\n      =(1+1/m)^m [a_m/(c_m-2)\n          +(1-a_m/(c_m-2))(2/c_m)^m] ->exp(1).\n\nHere a_m/(c_m-2)->1 and2/c_m is uniformly below1. Since\n(2^m-1)^p_0/lambda^m->1, (6) and (31) give (7). The upper comparison\nsequence tends to2gamma103/exp(1); no convergence of the actual supremum\nis assumed. Its bound is strictly below1 since gamma103<1 and exp(1)>2.\n\n## 10. Scope and what is now closed\n\nThis joint draft closes the original-H_m payoff comparison of EVERY\nparameter and EVERY checkpoint pair in C102 Section13, including both\nnearest-minimizer branches, u=0, positive reserves, arbitrary allowed\nintercepts, alpha and beta independent, and arbitrary nonnegative\nordinary block contents. The exact all-query realization and holding\nlimits are included, not assumed from the earlier unverified interface.\n\nThe reduction (14) is an actual legal whole-instance domination. The\nsubsequent comparison at (20)--(22) is an UPPER-RELAXATION comparison,\nnot a claim that deleting alpha keeps the terminal law legal. Confusing\nthese would turn a correct bound into an unsupported conversion claim.\nThe condition T>=sqrt(2)A belongs to (5); no all-word assertion removes\nit. All original H_m pairs satisfy it with room to spare.\n\nRemaining geometric restrictions are the orthogonality of p and r,\nthe sign pattern q=(-u,-v), the first/final plane intercepts fixed0,\nand two changes of gradient with constant physical blocks. Other\nendpoint angles, first/final relative intercepts, different sign\npatterns, or genuinely varying gradients inside ordinary blocks are\noutside the theorem. C99/C100 do not make these universal obstructions.\n\n## 11. Literature grounding and accounting\n\nMatlas search in active-program/repair mode returned the standard\nMoreau-gradient identity (Lemma4.1,arXiv2303.08785) and related generic\nMoreau results, but no original-word quantitative comparison. Web\ngrounding recovered Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,arXiv1502.05666. The exact\nlocal paper downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt was read at\nSection2.5,Theorem4,its proof/construction,and Corollary1. Its finite\nEuclidean smooth-convex regime specializes to smoothness1 here.\nNo new external theorem is a proof premise: Section2 proves the needed\nMoreau facts directly, and accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the project's\ndirected-row convention. The new algebraic comparison uses no external\nblack box. No additional literature regime is needed for this affine\nvariant of the already audited finite interpolation construction.\n\nAll mathematics is symbolic/textual. No numerical evaluation, solver,\nmachine algebra, replay, formal verifier, installation, consult or\nsubagent was used. No new fact or target completion is asserted.\n\n## 12. An actual acute endpoint-angle law outside the closed family\n\nHere is concrete static data for a distinct endpoint-angle hypothesis;\nit is not merely a Gram matrix or a proposed optimization problem. It\nworks on any actual two-checkpoint word as in Section1. Section14 decides\nits quantitative original-word comparison and shows that THIS safe\nparameter law also has no original-B gain.\n\nKeep T,M,N,A,E as above and set\n\n    zeta=E/2, B=E/2, D=M+zeta,\n    v=sqrt(A/(4D)),\n    u=min{A/(4D), Bv^2/(8N), N/B},\n    alpha=zeta u, beta=zeta v, kappa=zeta(u^2+v^2)/2,\n    delta=A-Du,\n    z=(C,V)=B(u,v), r=(C/(4N),V/(2N)),\n    p=e_1, q=(-u,-v),\n    J_ang(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},\n    F_ang(x)=min_y{J_ang(y)+||x-y||^2/2},\n    X_0=(T+delta,-Dv).                             (32)\n\nAll displayed u,v,B,D,C,V are positive. The final gradient has strictly\npositive first and second coordinates, so p and r form an ACUTE angle.\nThe middle and final gradients are also nonparallel: their component\nratios are v/u and2v/u. Formula(8) and its proof apply to this new finite\nmaximum as well.\n\nOn the first block the proposed proximal points are\n(delta+S_0-t,-Dv). Since Du<=A/4, delta>=3A/4, while Dv^2=A/4,\nthe first p-versus-q condition holds strictly. Also\nr_1=Bu/(4N)<=1/4, so\n<r,y><=r_1 y_1<=y_1 when y_1>=0 and y_2<0. The p-plane dominates\nzero too. Thus every first-block query has gradient p.\n\nThe first checkpoint sends X to Dq. Every middle proximal point is\n(zeta+S_1-t)q. Its q-plane height is at least\nzeta(u^2+v^2)-kappa=zeta(u^2+v^2)/2>0, whereas p,r have negative\nheight because their coordinates are nonnegative and q's are negative.\nThis proves all middle gradients q.\n\nThe second checkpoint sends X to z=B(u,v). Each tail proximal point is\n\n    y=([1-(1+t)/(4N)]C,[1-(1+t)/(2N)]V),\n    0<=t<=S_2=N-1.\n\nBoth coordinates are positive, with y_1<=C and y_2>=V/2. The choice\nu<=Bv^2/(8N) gives V^2>=8NC and therefore\n\n    <r,y>>=V^2/(4N)>=2C>=2y_1.\n\nHence the r-plane dominates the p-plane at every such y. It is positive\nwhile q's plane is negative, proving all tail gradients r and every\ndirected row by (10). All three ending-query affine reserves are\nstrictly positive: delta, zeta(u^2+v^2)/2,\n(3C^2+4V^2)/(16N).\n\nThe actual minimizer set is\nK_ang={x_1<=0,-u x_1-v x_2<=kappa,<r,x><=0}.\nEvery point has x_2>=-kappa/v because u>=0 and x_1<=0. Its vertical\nsegment x_1=0,-kappa/v<=x_2<=0 is included since r_2>0. The same\ncoordinatewise argument as Section2 therefore gives the TRUE closest\npoint and radius\n\n    x_star_ang=(0,-min(Dv,kappa/v)),\n    R_ang^2=(T+delta)^2+(Dv-kappa/v)_+^2. Their ratio is (35), and it is\nless than N because0<C<=N<4N. For the q-plane its gap is\n(r-q) dot y+kappa and its rate is ||r||^2-<q,r>>0. The maximizing inequalities also give\n\n    0<=F_k(x')-F_k(x)-<g(x),x'-x>\n       <=<g(x')-g(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.\n\nIt follows that F_k is finite, convex, differentiable and has gradient\ng. In particular it is globally1-smooth. If a plane of psi_k with slope\ny is active at g, then x=g+y has gradient g and value\n\n    F_k(x)=<x,g>-||g||^2/2-psi_k(g).                    (13)\n\nThe exact minimum value and set are\n\n    min F_k=0, argmin F_k=K_k:=conv{M_0,...,M_k}.        (14)\n\nFor x in K_k, <x,w><=max_j<M_j,w><=psi_k(w), so the conjugate is0,\nattained at w=0. Conversely, for x outside this compact convex set,\nlet m_0 be its closest point and d=x-m_0. The projection inequality\nimplies <d,M_j><=<d,m_0> and\n<x,d>><d,m_0>>=max_j<M_j,d>. At w=epsilon d for sufficiently small\nepsilon>0, the planes with intercepts-1,-2 are inactive. Hence\n\n    <x,epsilon d>-Phi_k(epsilon d)\n      =epsilon[<x,d>-max_j<M_j,d>]-epsilon^2||d||^2/2>0.\n\nThus F_k(x)>0, proving(14). The projection inequality used here follows\nby differentiating squared distance along each segment in K_k from\nm_0; expanding the square proves its sufficiency as well.\n\n### 3.2 All planes, all ordered rows, and the physical updates\n\nThe planar component of g_i is s_i p, where s_i=(-1)^i/P_i. We prove\nthe following complete support extrema among the minimum planes:\n\n    max_(0<=j<=i)<M_j,g_i>=<M_i,g_i>=H_i/P_i,\n    min_(0<=j<=i)<M_j,g_i>=-H_(i-1)/P_i.               (15)\n\nAt i=0 both are0. For j<i, orthogonality in(9) and(11) gives\n<M_j,g_i>=-<M_j,g_(i-1)>/t_i. Thus the old maximum becomes the new\nminimum -H_(i-1)/P_i, and the old minimum becomes H_(i-2)/P_i.\nThe new plane at its proposed gradient equals\n\n    <M_i,g_i>\n       =[||a_i||^2-<M_(i-1),g_(i-1)>]/t_i\n       =H_i/P_i.\n\nThis is at least H_(i-2)/P_i and at least0 by(8), proving(15).\nFor j>i, <M_j,g_i>=<M_i,g_i> because future increments are orthogonal\nto all previous coordinates. Thus(15) also bounds EVERY future plane.\n\nThe remaining fixed planes have values -5s_i-1 and2s_i-2 at g_i.\nWhen i is odd, their maximum together with0 is(5/P_i-1)_+;\nwhen i is even it is(2/P_i-2)_+. Equation(8) therefore proves\n\n    psi_k(g_i)=beta_i=<M_i,g_i>.                        (16)\n\nThe M_i plane is active, so(13) proves all new jets(11). At p,q,e,\nevery M plane vanishes. The zero plane is active at p, the nu plane\nat q, and the zero and2e planes at e. These give residuals0,nu,2e,e\nand all four absolute old values(1).\n\nFor completeness, formula(2) gives every ordered new/new row as\n\n    Q_ij=<M_j,g_i>-beta_i<=0.                          (17)\n\nFor a new i and the four old jets, in order(1), its four rows are\n\n    -beta_i, -5s_i-1-beta_i,\n    2s_i-2-beta_i, s_i-1-beta_i.                        (18)\n\nThey are nonpositive by(8),(16); the fourth follows from the maximum\nof0 and2s_i-2. Each reversed old/new row is0 because every M_i is\nperpendicular to E and <M_i,g_i>=beta_i. Old/old rows are exactly those\nof62a7d9329121fd75. For every m in K_k the new/minimum row is\n<m,g_i>-beta_i<=0, while the reverse row is0. For the four old jets\ntheir rows to any m are0; the reverse rows are0,-1,-2,-1.\nThus no old, new, terminal or minimum row in either orientation is lost.\n\nThe actual recursion is an identity:\n\n    X_i-c_i g_i=M_i-t_i g_i\n       =M_(i-1)+g_(i-1)=X_(i-1).                      (19)\n\nIn particular no unproved inverse-map assertion is used.\n\nSince M_i maximizes <m,g_i> on K_k, for all m in K_k,\n<g_i,m-M_i><=0. Expanding ||M_i+g_i-m||^2 shows its minimum is\n||g_i||^2, attained at M_i. This proves the TRUE radius assertion in(12),\nalso for earlier queried points after all future planes have been added.\nFinally orthogonality gives\n\n    D_i=[D_(i-1)+(H_(i-1)+H_i)/P_(i-1)]/t_i^2.\n\nMultiplication by P_i^2 and summation, starting from D_0=13, proves(12).\nThe case k=1,t_1=1 gives ||a_1||^2=4, K_1=[0,a_1], D_1=17,\nand exactly the equality data(6), completing Section2's attainment.\n\n### 3.3 Exact optimality within the declared orthogonal policy\n\nThe family restriction in this subsection is explicit. Retain(1), use\nthe SAME form(10), choose arbitrary fresh orthogonal increments a_i,\nrequire residual M_i at each new gradient, use the physical steps c_i,\nand retain every intermediate jet. A member is admissible only if all\nthese are actual jets of its static function. No unrestricted interpolant\nor arbitrary nonorthogonal choice is included in this family assertion.\n\nFor an admissible member write its height numerator as J_i=P_i beta_i,\nwith J_(-1)=J_0=0. Its residual M_i is a subgradient of psi_k at g_i.\nThe subgradient inequality evaluated at0, where psi_k(0)=0, gives\npsi_k(g_i)<=<M_i,g_i>. The reverse inequality holds because the\nM_i plane is one of the defining planes. Thus that plane must be\nactive. The support-extrema argument proving(15) applies and forces\n\n    J_i>=J_(i-2),\n    J_i>=(5-P_i)_+ for odd i,\n    J_i>=(2-2P_i)_+ for even i,\n    ||a_i||^2=(J_(i-1)+J_i)/P_(i-1).                   (20)\n\nConversely these inequalities make the same proof of(15)-(19) work.\nThey therefore characterize this declared family exactly, including\nall cross-history rows. Induction gives J_i>=H_i from(8), coordinate\nby coordinate. Its squared radius is exactly the expression(12) with\nH replaced by J, all of whose coefficients are nonnegative. Hence(8)\nattains the MINIMUM true radius over the entire declared policy, even\nif larger earlier increments are chosen with knowledge of the full\nword. No hidden greedy-optimality or continuity assumption is required.\n\n## 4. A variable large-step family with linear paid radius\n\nFix an integer m>=1 and t in[3/4,1]. Put\n\n    a=1+1/t, b=1+t, A=a+b=2+t+1/t,\n    C(t)=(1+t)(5-t)=5+4t-t^2,\n    h^(m,t)=((a,b) repeated m times,2,2,1).             (21)\n\nThe physical horizon is exactly n=2m+3. For t<1, each pair has one\nstep above2 and one strictly between1 and2. These are actual steps,\nnot altered enclosing parameters.\n\nUse k=2m and the backward t_i alternately t,1/t. Then P_i=t for odd\ni and1 for even i, H_i=5-t for odd i and0 for even i. Thus each odd\nincrement has squared norm5-t and each even one(5-t)/t. All are\npositive. Formula(12) becomes\n\n    D_(2m)=13+m C(t),\n    V_(m,t)=3/[13+m C(t)].                             (22)\n\nThe gradients change at every new query because a new nonzero orthogonal\ncoordinate is added. Equations(8)-(19) specify its full static function,\nabsolute jets, heights, minimum polytope and all rows. In particular(22)\nis an attained true-distance payoff, not just a feasible Gram score.\n\n## 5. Complete SAME-word alternating-ray comparisons\n\nWe use the COMPLETE channel in1fca3e5dd555cb4a, with its specified\ninitial point1 and minimizer0. For a checkpoint decomposition of a word,\nwrite the ordinary block sums as s_j and selected steps as b_j. With\nell>=1 checkpoints, its magnitudes and remote offsets obey\n\n    m_0=1/(1+s_0), O_0=0,\n    m_(j+1)=[(b_j-1)m_j-O_j]/(1+s_(j+1)),\n    O_(j+1)=O_j+s_j m_j.\n\nThey must satisfy m_j>0 and m_j>=m_(j-2) where applicable. At the\nlast checkpoint put z_term=(b_(ell-1)-1)m_(ell-1)-O_(ell-1), and\nm_old=m_(ell-2) if ell>=2, otherwise0. Feasibility includes\n\n    z_term>=(1+s_ell)m_old.                             (23)\n\nThe payoff is the maximum of\n2 e_term+2q z_term-(2s_ell+1)q^2 over the accepted interval\n[m_old,z_term/(1+s_ell)], where e_term is the sum of s_j m_j^2\nover0<=j<ell with j congruent to ell modulo2. No value of this\naccumulated area can cure an empty interval(23). Empty checkpoints give1/(1+2S), where\nS is total mass. A singleton step h_j>=1 gives exactly\n\n    (h_j-1)^2/[(1+Psum_j)^2(1+2Tsum_j)],               (24)\n\nwhere Psum_j and Tsum_j are the sums before and after that step.\nSingleton steps below1 are infeasible; the zero-payoff step1 is allowed.\nF_ray is the maximum over ALL feasible choices. K is precisely the\nmaximum of the empty and singleton values with positive parts.\n\nFor(21), a selected final step1 makes any multi-checkpoint set infeasible:\nz_term=-O<=0 while m_old>0. Otherwise the unselected terminal mass is\nat least1. Every selected step minus1 is at most4/3; all offsets are\nnonnegative. Thus, for ANY multi-checkpoint set,\n\n    z_term<=(4/3)m_(ell-1)<=(16/9)m_old\n             <2m_old<=(1+s_ell)m_old.\n\nThis excludes all such sets by the full terminal constraint.\n\nHere the first step a>=2 is also the largest physical step. A useful\nexplicit denominator comparison eliminates every later singleton. For\nany step h with preceding sum P and following sum T, let S=P+h+T and\nC0=1+2(S-a). Direct expansion gives\n\n    (1+P)^2(1+2T)-C0\n       =P[(P+2)(1+2T)-2]+2(a-h)>=0.                   (25)\n\nIts numerator(h-1)_+^2 is at most(a-1)^2. The empty value is smaller\nthan the first cut because(a-1)^2>=1 and C0<1+2S. Consequently the\nCOMPLETE same-word references are exactly\n\n    F_ray_(2m+3)(h^(m,t))=K_(2m+3)(h^(m,t))\n       =1/[t^2(2m A+11-2a)].                          (26)\n\nThe new payoff exceeds(26) for every stated m,t. Clearing the positive\ndenominators reduces the strict comparison to\n\n    m(6t^3+13t^2+2t-5)+(27t^2-6t-13)>0.               (27)\n\nBoth parenthesized polynomials increase on[3/4,1]. At3/4 the first\nis203/32 and the second is-37/16. Thus the left side is at least\n129/32 for m>=1. The exact ratio and its limit are\n\n    V_(m,t)/F_ray=3t^2(2m A+11-2a)/[13+m C(t)],\n    lim_(m->infinity) V_(m,t)/F_ray\n       =6t(t+1)^2/[(1+t)(5-t)]>1.                    (28)\n\nAt t=1 this recovers limit3, but(21)-(28) include a continuum of\nnonconstant physical words.\n\n## 6. Full accepted complex-polynomial reference: necessary suffix cycles\n\nWe keep exactly the accepted reference dfde2fa326c908bf. For a complex\nz=alpha+i beta, its points are xi_j=Poly_j(z), where\nPoly_j(z)=product_(l<j)(1-h_l z); its gradients are gamma_j=z xi_j,\nand xi_*=gamma_*=0. For every directed index-simple cycle define\nArea_C=sum Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) and Energy_C=sum|xi_i-xi_j|^2 over\nits directed edges j->i. The FULL legal set D_h requires\n\n    (alpha-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C|\n\nfor every such cycle. The accepted Gap_h(z) is the largest normalized\nterminal value for those exact points and gradients, with initial\npoint1, specified minimizer0 and denominator1. The accepted estimate is\n\n    0<=Gap_h(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2.            (29)\n\nNo closest-minimum renormalization or universal-lens substitution is\nmade for this reference.\n\nEvery word used below ends in the actual suffix(2,2,1). Put\n\n    w=1-2z=d+i v, sigma=|w|^2, delta=1-sigma.\n\nThe optimum/index0 two-cycle forces alpha-|z|^2>=0, equivalently\n0<=sigma<=1. If the prefix polynomial at the start of the suffix is0,\nthe terminal polynomial is0 and(29) gives Gap_h=0. Otherwise the\nsuffix starts at a nonzero common complex scale. The two actual cycles\nwith suffix positions(0,1,w) and(1,w,w^2), with0 denoting the optimum,\nhave areas v and v|1-w|^2 and energies\n2(1+sigma-d) and2|1-w|^2(1+sigma+d), up to the SAME positive scale\nfactor. If w=1, z=0 and the gap is0. In all other cases division gives\n\n    2v^2<=delta(1+sigma-|d|),\n    d^2>=(sigma^2+2sigma-1)/2.                         (30)\n\nAll these cycles use distinct sample indices even when coordinates\ncoincide. They are necessary constraints on the full legal set, not\na replacement definition of it.\n\nThe contribution of the last(2,2,1) to the terminal bound is exactly\n\n    T(z)=(2alpha-|z|^2)|w^2(1+w)/2|^2\n        =sigma^2[4-(2d+sigma-1)^2]/16.                 (31)\n\nTwo uniform consequences of(30) will be used:\n\n    T(z)<=4/25,\n    T(z)<=(5/4)sigma^2 delta.                          (32)\n\nFor the first, if sigma<=4/5, (31)<=sigma^2/4<=4/25. Otherwise(30)\ngives d^2>=31/50>9/16. If d>=3/4, then2d+sigma-1>=13/10 and\nT<=231/1600<4/25. If d<=-3/4, then2d+sigma-1<=-3/2 and\nT<=7/64<4/25. This proves the first bound.\n\nFor the second, let the bracket in(31) be J. When delta>=1/5,\nJ<=4<=20delta. When0<=delta<=1/5, (30) implies\nd^2>=1-2delta+delta^2/2>=(1-2delta)^2. If d>=1-2delta,\n2d-delta>=2-5delta>=0, so J<=20delta-25delta^2<=20delta.\nIf d<=-1+2delta, then2d-delta<=-2+3delta<0, so\nJ<=12delta-9delta^2<=20delta. Substitution in(31) proves(32).\n\n## 7. Full complex comparison for EVERY reciprocal-pair word(21)\n\nFor(21), the actual pair polynomial is\n\n    Q(z)=(1-a z)(1-b z)=1-A z+A z^2\n        =gamma w^2-(gamma-1),\n    gamma=A/4 in[1,49/48].                            (33)\n\nIndeed ab=A, and A is decreasing on[3/4,1] from49/12 to4. The full\nterminal bound in(29) is |Q(z)|^(2m) T(z). We bound this over EVERY\nz in the full D_h, using(30), including all boundary cases.\n\nIf delta>=1/5, sigma<=4/5, and\n\n    |Q|<=gamma sigma+gamma-1<=67/80,\n    |Q|^2<3/4.\n\nThus the gap is at most(4/25)(3/4)^m. For integers m>=1,\n\n    (13+8m)(3/4)^m<=261/16.
