Hence\n\n    (4/25)(3/4)^m<3/(13+8m)<=V_(m,t),                 (35)\n\nwhere C(t)<=8 and(4/25)(261/16)=261/100<3 were used.\n\nIf0<=delta<=1/5, the second condition in(30) gives\n\n    |Q|^2<=1-2gamma(3-2gamma)delta\n                  +gamma(2-gamma)delta^2\n           <=1-(7/4)delta.                            (36)\n\nFor an explicit verification of the last coefficient, gamma(2-gamma)\nis positive on the stated interval, and\n\n    2gamma(3-2gamma)-gamma(2-gamma)delta\n       >=gamma(28-19gamma)/5\n       >=20237/11520>7/4.\n\nThe middle quadratic decreases on[1,49/48]; its displayed value is\nat49/48. Also sigma^2=(1-delta)^2<=1-(7/4)delta for delta<=1/5.\nCombining(32),(36), putting x=7delta/4, and maximizing on[0,1] yields\n\n    Gap_h(z)<=(5/4)delta[1-(7/4)delta]^(m+1)\n       <=5/[7(m+2)] * ((m+1)/(m+2))^(m+1)\n       <=5/[14(m+2)]\n       <3/(13+8m)<=V_(m,t).                           (37)\n\nThe maximum follows by differentiating x(1-x)^(m+1); the factor at\nits maximum is at most1/2 since the first two terms of\n(1+1/(m+1))^(m+1) already sum to2. The last strict comparison is\n65+40m<84+42m. The case delta=0 gives zero gap by(31),(30).\n\nEquations(35),(37) are uniform in z. In particular\n\n    sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z)\n      <=max{(4/25)(3/4)^m,5/[14(m+2)]}<V_(m,t).         (38)\n\nThis bounds the FULL accepted channel with all its cycles and legal\nvalues. It also bounds every scalar quadratic, since its curvature\ntheta in[0,1] gives a legal real z=theta and its actual quadratic\nvalue is no larger than Gap_h(theta). An orthogonal sum of convex\nquadratics has a normalized payoff which is a nonnegative weighted\naverage of the scalar payoffs, including zero-curvature components;\ntherefore that entire quadratic class is dominated as well. No claim\nabout every nonlinear scalar function is made.\n\n## 8. An actual short/large TWO-step prepend on one static function\n\nThere is also a genuinely mixed construction containing a step below1.\nFix u in[1/2,1] and define the EXACT five-step word\n\n    h^(u)=(1-u,1+1/u,2,2,1).                           (39)\n\nLet a be perpendicular to E with||a||=2, set g=-p+a, and use the\nsingle three-dimensional function\n\n    psi(w)=max{0,<nu,w>-1,2<e,w>-2,<a,w>},\n    F(x)=sup_w{<x,w>-||w||^2/2-psi(w)}.                 (40)\n\nThis is(10) with one minimum increment. Its exact minimum set is[0,a]\nand its minimum value is0 by Section3.1. Define two new queried jets\n\n    X=a+g=-p+2a,       gradient g,      value17/2,\n    Z=a+u g,          gradient u g,    value17u^2/2.    (41)\n\nAt g the a-plane has value4 and is active. At u g it has value4u,\nwhile the nu-plane is5u-1<=4u, the2e plane is-2u-2, and the zero\nplane is0. Hence it is active there too, and(13) proves(41).\nBoth new residuals equal a; their heights are4 and4u. The updates are\n\n    X-(1-u)g=Z,\n    Z-(1+1/u)(u g)=a-g=p.                             (42)\n\nThe remainder is exactly(1). For u<1, these are genuinely changing\ngradients g,u g,p. At u=1 the first step is the actual zero step and\nits duplicate jet remains in the physical five-step word.\n\nAll ordered rows are explicit. The new/new rows in both directions\nare0. For Z to the four old jets they are\n\n    -4u, u-1, -6u-2, -5u-1,\n\nand for X they are these expressions at u=1. All reversed old/new\nrows are0. For every minimum lambda a with0<=lambda<=1, the new/minimum\nrows are4(lambda-1) and4u(lambda-1), while their reverses are0.\nThe old/minimum and old/old rows are those already proved in Section3.2.\nThus the inserted short step does not omit its absolute value or any\nnew/old directed constraint.\n\nThe closest minimizer to X is a, since <g,lambda a-a>=4(lambda-1)<=0.\nIts TRUE initial squared radius is17, and its actual terminal payoff is\n\n    V_mix=3/17,                                        (43)\n\nfor EVERY u in[1/2,1]. The function(40) itself is independent of u;\nthe intermediate point and its absolute value vary with the word.\n\n### 8.1 Complete same-word ray reference\n\nA step1-u<1 cannot occur in a feasible checkpoint set: it either makes\nthe next required magnitude negative or makes the final z_term negative.\nA final step1 also makes every multi-checkpoint set infeasible; its\nsingleton value is0. Remaining possible checkpoints are c=1+1/u and\nthe two old steps2. Their multipliers b_j-1 are at most2.\n\nIf the last checkpoint is the first old2, its predecessor can only be c;\nz_term<=2m_old, while the unselected tail has mass3 and(23) requires\nat least4m_old. If the last checkpoint is the second old2, and its\npredecessor is the first old2, then z_term<=m_old<2m_old. If its\npredecessor is c instead, the unselected intervening step2 gives\nm_(ell-1)<=2m_old/3, again contradicting the required2m_old. The last\ncheckpoint cannot be c in a multi-checkpoint set because none before\nit is admissible. This treats ALL multiple checkpoints.\n\nThe empty value is1/(15+2/u-2u)<=1/15. The c singleton has value\n\n    1/[11u^2(2-u)^2]>=1/11.\n\nThe two old2 singletons are\n1/[7(3+1/u-u)^2]<=1/63 and1/[3(5+1/u-u)^2]<=1/75. Therefore\n\n    F_ray_5(h^(u))=K_5(h^(u))=1/[11u^2(2-u)^2],\n    V_mix/F_ray=33u^2(2-u)^2/17>=297/272>1.            (44)\n\nThe last bound uses that u(2-u) increases on[1/2,1], with minimum3/4.\n\n### 8.2 The full same-word complex reference\n\nKeep the notation and necessary suffix cycles of Section6. The actual\ntwo-step prefix polynomial in(39) is\n\n    Q_mix(z)=(1-(1-u)z)(1-(1+1/u)z)\n       =w+rho(w^2-1), rho=(1/u-u)/4 in[0,3/8].         (45)\n\nIf Q_mix=0, the gap is0. Otherwise(30) applies. Direct expansion gives\n\n    |Q_mix|^2=sigma+rho^2(delta^2+4v^2)-2rho d delta.\n\nUse2v^2<=delta(1+sigma-|d|) from(30), then |d|<=1, sigma<=1 and\n0<=rho<=3/8. This gives\n\n    |Q_mix|^2\n      <=1+delta[-1+rho^2(3+sigma)+2rho(1-rho)|d|]\n      <=1+delta[-1+2rho+2rho^2]\n      <=1+delta/32<=33/32.                             (46)\n\nThe polynomial in rho increases on the stated interval and equals1/32\nat3/8. Combining the actual terminal bound |Q_mix|^2 T(z) with(32),\n\n    sup_(z in D_(h^(u))) Gap_(h^(u))(z)\n       <=33/200<3/17=V_mix.                            (47)\n\nThe strict comparison is561<600 after multiplying by3400. Thus EVERY\nword(39), including its zero-step endpoint, beats the complete accepted\nray and full complex references, as well as scalar and orthogonal-sum\nquadratic objectives by the argument following(38). This is not a\ncomparison with all nonlinear scalar trajectories.\n\n## 9. Original benchmark and exact limits of the continuation policy\n\nUse exactly the original benchmark quantities C_n=1+S_circle(n) and\nB_n=1+2S_bullet(n) defined in b98bb807d514e80d. Their mass recurrence is\n\n    B_0=C_0=1,\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.\n\nChoosing j=0 gives B_n>=G(1,B_(n-1))>=B_(n-1)+2. Induction proves\nB_n>=2n+1. Thus for the actual horizon n=2m+3 of(21),\n\n    B_n V_(m,t)>=3(4m+7)/(13+8m)>1.                   (48)\n\nFor(39) the actual horizon is5, even when u=1, and B_5 V_mix>=33/17>1.\nBoth diagnostic families therefore already give a lower bound above\nthe original reciprocal profile on their OWN physical words.\n\nThis does not supply the original all-word theorem. There is a complete\nscoped obstruction for the entire policy of Section3.3, not only for\none bad choice of its increments. Fix ANY t in(0,1), and take all k\nprepended steps equal to1+t. Its products are P_i=t^i, so(12),(20) give\nfor the MINIMUM possible policy radius and the MAXIMUM policy payoff\n\n    D_k>=13/t^(2k),\n    V_policy<=3t^(2k)/13.                              (49)\n\nThe ordinary Huber reference on exactly this word is\n1/[2k(1+t)+11]. Therefore\n\n    V_policy/Huber\n       <=[3(2k(1+t)+11)/13]t^(2k) ->0.                 (50)\n\nThe actual horizon is k+3. Using the ACCEPTED bound\nB_(k+3)<=1+4(k+3)^p, p=log_2(3), also gives\n\n    B_(k+3) V_policy\n       <=(3/13)[1+4(k+3)^p]t^(2k) ->0.                 (51)\n\nThese limits follow, for example, because the successive ratios of the\npositive polynomial-times-t^(2k) expressions tend to t^2<1; eventually\nthey are bounded above by one fixed number less than1. The bounds hold\neven when all increments in the declared policy are chosen with\ncomplete knowledge of the word, by the exact optimality proof(20).\nThe case t=1/2 already supplies a constant-step3/2 obstruction within\nthat policy. No assertion about small unrestricted W is implied.\n\nA unit step exposes a separate exact family boundary. Whenever a new\nqueried point has residual equal to an actual minimizer M, it is X=M+g.\nA physical step1 sends it to M. It cannot reach any of the nonminimal\nold/new endpoints under discussion. This removes the previous6w+7v<=18 restriction; remaining threshold search requires w>4/3.", "evidence": "# Nested terminal triangles exclude the entire small-second-step region\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Statement, scope, and accepted inputs\n\nThe regional assertion proved in this draft is:\n\nFor every four-step word h=(u,w,z,v) with nonnegative entries,\n\n    u+w+z+v >= 168/25,       w <= 4/3,                 (1)\n\nthere is no original primitive certificate with all twenty nonnegative\nfinite multipliers, exact flow, and a matrix positive semidefinite on\nthe full five-gradient space. The first, third, and last steps have no\nadditional prescribed intervals. No ordering, reversal, or coordinate\npermutation is used.\n\nIf the four old cut conditions and the old terminal-triangle condition\nhold, the proof supplies one explicit static function depending on h,\nof dimension at most three, whose primitive energy is\n\n    V_h < -961/4375.                                  (2)\n\nThis removes the previous restriction 6w+7v<=18 from the round39 draft.\nIt covers its actual complement within w<=4/3, and does not merely fix\nthe first step or perturb the former physical box.\n\nOnly accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, and accepted1252b8823bb5a290\nare inputs. The first supplies the exact original-array identity; the\nsecond supplies the interpolation orientation. All functions, every\nphysical update, all directed rows, and all parameter comparisons\nneeded below are proved jointly here. Neither unverified8d4369993526363e\nnor any earlier unverified triangle assertion is a correctness premise.\n\nAudit8d78e0975df8400a and guidance1501c682ec2aff49 have been read. The\noriginal scalar draft43a616da7da451c7 remains defective as printed; its\noriginal file and hash are preserved. No scalar first-increment bound,\nglobal domain reduction, Bernstein table, broad row-redundancy claim,\nor whole-box assertion from that package is used here. This document\ndoes not purport to repair or accept that separate scalar package.\n\n## 2. The full original certificate and a realization device\n\nPut s=u+w+z+v and C=1+s. Original finite indices are0,1,2,3,4.\nLet Lambda_ij>=0 for all twenty distinct finite pairs and Lambda_ii=0.\nLet e_all=(1,1,1,1,1)^T and define\n\n    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e_all)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2u,-2w,-2z,-2v,2s)^T,\n    Dprim=diag(u(2-u),w(2-w),z(2-z),v(2-v),-s(s+2)),\n    M=Dprim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.\n\nThe proposed certificate requires L_flow^T e_all=omega and M PSD on\nall of R^5. Original gradients at zero steps are not identified or\ndeleted. For actual positions X_i, gradients G_i, and values F_i set\n\n    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(F_i-F_4)\n         +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2-sC||G_4||^2.\n\nThe accepted exact identity is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(G_0,...,G_4)>.(3)\n\nThe inner product of matrices is trace of the transpose times the\nsecond matrix. Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0.\nThus a negative actual V_h contradicts the entire original certificate.\n\nAll witnesses here use saturated values\n\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                       (4)\n\nTheir residuals and primitive energy simplify exactly to\n\n    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>,\n    V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_4+sG_4||^2.                  (5)\n\nFor the second identity, 2sum h_i<X_i,G_i>-sum h_i^2||G_i||^2\ntelescopes to ||X_0||^2-||X_4||^2 under the original updates; the\nremaining terms are -2s<X_4,G_4>-s^2||G_4||^2.\n\nHere is the static realization used below, including the optimum.\nSuppose K is the convex hull of the displayed gradients, contains0,\nand every source vertex satisfies <G-G_j,X_j-G_j><=0. Define\n\n    F_K(X)=max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).             (6)\n\nCompleting the square identifies the unique maximizer as the Euclidean\nprojection P_K(X). The normal inequalities imply P_K(X_j)=G_j, so\nthe function has precisely(4) at each original query. Two projection\ninequalities, added, give\n\n    <X-X',P_K(X)-P_K(X')> >= ||P_K(X)-P_K(X')||^2.\n\nHence P_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities give\n\n    0<=F_K(X')-F_K(X)-<P_K(X),X'-X>\n       <=<P_K(X')-P_K(X),X'-X><=||X'-X||^2.\n\nThis proves differentiability and gradient P_K. The function is convex\nas a maximum of affine functions, and finite because K is compact.\nSince0 is in K, F_K>=0=F_K(0). In particular it has a true minimizer.\nNo uniqueness or normalized objective payoff is asserted. For(4), all\nQ_(i,star) vanish; Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,X_i-G_i> will also be checked.\n\n## 3. The older cuts and the terminal triangle, freshly derived\n\nFor j in{0,1,2,3}, let Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i and Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i.\nEvery full certificate obeys\n\n    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).                     (7)\n\nWhen h_j>1, put A=1+Pj,B=1+Rj,r=(h_j-1)/B. The function(6) with\nK=[-r,1], started at X_0=A, has gradient1 throughout the prefix up\nto j. The selected update moves X_j=1 to -Br. The remaining gradients\nare all -r, and X_4=G_4=-r. Every original zero step is included.\nEquation(5) gives V_h=A^2-C^2r^2, proving(7). When h_j<=1 the cut\nis automatic.\n\nWrite\n\n    m=1+w, N=u+m, D=1+v, c=z-1, d=v-1.\n\nThen C=N+D+c. In our threshold region, the endpoint cuts give\n\n    u,v <= 2C/(C+1) < 2. Thus c>0 is derived, not imposed as a reduction.\n\nWhen v>1, the terminal adjacent pair also requires cd<1. If cd>=1,\nuse the one-dimensional function with derivative min(x,1), namely\nF(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1 and F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1. Its initial point N\ngives the exact queries\n\n    X=(N,m,1,-c,cd),       G=(1,1,1,-c,1).\n\nAll values are(4), the function has minimum0, and(5) gives\n\n    V_h=N^2-(N+zv)^2<0.                             (10)\n\nNow assume v>1 and cd<1. Define\n\n    E=Dd=v^2-1,\n    Ktri=D+c(1-E)=c^2 d+(c+D)(1-cd)>0,\n    Rtri=c^2 d/Ktri,   t=c/D,\n    e=(1,0), r=(Rtri,sqrt(Rtri(1-Rtri))),\n    p=-(c e+r)/v,   n=-c e-p,\n    Btri=(cd-Rtri)/v.                               (11)\n\nHere0<Rtri<1 and Btri>0: Ktri-c=D(1-cd)>0 implies cd>Rtri.\nDirect inner products give\n\n    <e,r>=||r||^2=Rtri,  <e,p>=-(c+Rtri)/v<0,\n    <e,n>=-Btri,   <p,n>=<r,n>=t Btri.               (12)\n\nFor the last identity, expand p and n and use Ktri Rtri=c^2 d;\nequivalently <p,n>=Rtri(1-cd)/v and\nRtri(1-cd)/(cd-Rtri)=c/D=t.\n\nTake the original base trajectory\n\n    Xbase=(N e,m e,e,-c e,r),\n    Gbase=(e,e,e,p,r).                               (13)\n\nAll physical updates hold because -c e-vp=r. Its nonzero target\nnormals are (N-1)e at0, w e at1, and n at3. At0 and1 the e-component\nof each other vertex is at most1. At3, the comparisons from e and r\nare respectively -(1+t)Btri and0 by(12). The other target normals are\nzero. This checks all ordered finite rows. Also\nc e+v p+r=0, with positive coefficients, so0 belongs to conv{e,p,r}.\nThe optimum products are N-1,w,0,t Btri,0, all nonnegative.\nThus(6) is one legal static function realizing(13), and(5) yields\n\n    V_h=N^2-C^2 Rtri.\n\nThe older terminal-triangle necessity, on its proved domain, is\n\n    cd<1,       C^2 Rtri<=N^2       when v>1.         (14)\n\nNothing from an unexamined global domain reduction was used.\n\n## 4. The enlarged nested family and every original row\n\nThis section is valid for all u>1,w>=0,z>1,v>1 with cd<1, without\nassuming a threshold. Put\n\n    a=u-1, q=a/m>0, t=c/D>0,\n\nand use all triangle quantities from(11). Given rho>=0, form vectors\nin the direct sum of two Euclidean planes:\n\n    X_0=(N e,sqrt(rho)e),       G_0=(e,sqrt(rho)e),\n    X_1=(m e,-sqrt(rho)a e),    G_1=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),\n    X_2=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),      G_2=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),\n    X_3=(-c e,sqrt(rho)cq e),   G_3=(p,-sqrt(rho)q p),\n    X_4=(r,-sqrt(rho)q r),      G_4=(r,-sqrt(rho)q r). (15)\n\nAll vectors lie in the sum of the two-dimensional subspace\n{(y,-sqrt(rho)q y): y in R^2} and the line generated by(e,sqrt(rho)e).\nThus the function and trajectory can be placed in dimension at most3.\nThe use of direct-sum coordinates is just explicit notation, not a\nrestriction on the full original certificate.\n\nThe first auxiliary update is1-u=-a, the second is-a+wq=-q because\na=mq, the third is-q+zq=cq, and the fourth iscq e+vq p=-q r.\nThe base updates were checked in(13). Every step retains its original\nlength. Assign the values(4).\n\nThe complete target normals are\n\n    Y_0=((N-1)e,0), Y_1=(w e,-sqrt(rho)wq e),\n    Y_2=0, Y_3=(n,-sqrt(rho)q n), Y_4=0.\n\nHere is the exhaustive finite-row list, grouped by target:\n\n* Target0: sources1,2 give0; source3 gives\n  (N-1)(<e,p>-1)<0; source4 gives(N-1)(Rtri-1)<0.\n* Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q)<=0; source2 gives0;\n  sources3,4 respectively give\n  w*(1+rho*q^2)(<e,p>-1)<=0 and\n  w*(1+rho*q^2)(Rtri-1)<=0.\n* Targets2 and4: every source gives0.\n* Target3: sources1,2 give-(1+rho*q^2)(1+t)Btri<0;\n  source4 gives0; source0 gives precisely\n\n      Q_03=Btri[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-qt)].                (16)\n\nThus all twenty original finite rows hold if and only if\n\n    0<=rho<=(1+t)/(q(1-qt))       when qt<1,\n    arbitrary rho>=0             when qt>=1.         (17)\n\nThis includes qt=1 and rho=0. Necessity is the single row(16), since\nBtri>0. No other pair is deleted. The optimum products <G_i,Y_i> are\n\n    N-1, w(1+rho*q^2), 0, t Btri(1+rho*q^2), 0.\n\nThey are nonnegative; hence every optimum row holds too. Moreover\ncG_1+vG_3+G_4=0, so the static function(6) for\n\n    K_nested=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3,G_4}                  (18)\n\nhas minimum0 and realizes every sample. It is one fixed function for\nthis word and this amplitude, not a different function at each query.\nIt is not asserted to be a common function independent of the word.\n\nIts exact energy for every allowed amplitude is\n\n    V_h(rho)=N^2-C^2 Rtri+rho*(1-C^2 q^2 Rtri).     (19)\n\nThis is the complete amplitude test for this explicit family on the\nstated domain. At qt<1 it suffices and is necessary to test both\nendpoints of(17), since(19) is affine. At qt>=1 it is equivalent to\nN^2-C^2 Rtri>=0 and1-C^2 q^2 Rtri>=0. These equivalences concern\nthis family alone, not all legal functions or all certificates.\n\nFor qt<1 choose the upper endpoint in(17), and define\n\n    k=(qN^2-1/q)/(1+q)\n      =u*(N-m/(u-1))=u^2+m*u-m-m/(u-1).\n\nExpanding(19) after multiplication by1-qt gives\n\n    V_h=(1+q)/(1-qt) * Gtri,\n    Gtri=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2 Rtri.                      (20)\n\nIndeed the coefficient of C^2 Rtri becomes-(1+q), and the remaining\nnumerator is (1+q)N^2-(1+t)(qN^2-1/q).\n\n## 5. The planar member needed below v=sqrt(2)\n\nFor u>1,w>=0,z>1,v>=0, put q=(u-1)/m,t=c/D and use scalar base data\n\n    Xbase=(N,m,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),\n    Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq),\n    X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)Xaux_i),\n    G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)Gaux_i).\n\nThe four updates are N-u=m,m-w=1,1-z=-c,-c+vt=-t, and\n1-u=-a,-a+wq=-q,-q+zq=cq,cq-vtq=tq. Use(4).\nThe base normals are(N-1,w,0,-vt,0), and the auxiliary normals are\n(0,-wq,0,vtq,0). The complete finite rows are:\n\n* Target0: sources1,2 give0 and sources3,4 give-(1+t)(N-1).\n* Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q); source2 gives0;\n  sources3,4 give-w*(1+t)(1+rho*q^2).\n* Targets2,4: all sources give0.\n* Target3: sources1,2 give-vt*(1+t)(1+rho*q^2); source4 gives0;\n  source0 gives vt[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-tq)].\n\nAll optimum products are N-1,w(1+rho*q^2),0,\nvt^2(1+rho*q^2),0. Also G_4=-tG_1, so0 is in the hull of\nG_0,G_1,G_4. The function(6) realizes these data.\n\nThe full allowed amplitude is(17) when v>0 and tq<1, and arbitrary\nrho>=0 when v=0 or tq>=1. In particular, whenever0<tq<1 the capped\nrho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq)) is legal even at v=0. At that amplitude(5) gives\n\n    V_h=(1+q)/(1-tq) * Gcut,\n    Gcut=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2 t^2.                       (21)\n\nThis is rederived here; no fixed trajectory is extrapolated across\nan unchecked phase.\n\n## 6. Two elementary tail formulas\n\nPut lambda=1+sqrt(2), so lambda(lambda-2)=1, and let L=C-N denote\nthe sum c+D in this section only. At fixed D and c=L-D, write\n\n    R(L,D)=c^2(D-2)/[D+c(1-D(D-2))].                (22)\n\nFor D>=lambda and cd<1 its denominator is positive. With\nE=D(D-2)>=1 and chi=1-(E-1)t in(0,1], equation(22) reads\n\n    R=t^2 E/chi,\n    R_c=E*t*(1+chi)/(D*chi^2) >= 2t/D >0.           (23)\n\nFor the inequality, (1+chi)/chi^2>=2 follows from\n1+chi-2chi^2=(1-chi)(1+2chi)>=0. In the planar case Rcut=t^2,\nthe corresponding derivative is exactly2t/D.\n\nFor any fixed0<rbar<1, the inequality R(L,D)>rbar is equivalent,\non a positive denominator, to\n\n    F_rbar(D):=(D-2)(L-D)(L-(1-rbar)D)-rbar*L >0.   (24)\n\nIf L>=18/5 and lambda<=D<=14/5, its second derivative satisfies\n\n    F_rbar''=6(1-rbar)D-2L(2-rbar)-4(1-rbar)\n        <=[64(1-rbar)-36(2-rbar)]/5\n         =-(8+28rbar)/5<0.                         (25)\n\nTherefore positive endpoint values imply positivity throughout the\nclosed interval, by concavity. This avoids assuming R is monotone\nin D; it need not be. At D=lambda, its exact value is\n\n    R(L,lambda)=(L-lambda)^2/lambda^2.               (26)\n\n## 7. Every possible old survivor in(1) enters the proved phase\n\nAssume all cuts(7) and the terminal condition(14) hold. First v<9/5.\nIndeed if v>=9/5, the last cut implies C>=v/(2-v)>=9. By(8),\nN<13/3 and D<3, so c=C-N-D>5/3 and d>=4/5. Thus cd>4/3,\ncontradicting(14). This used no capacity upper bound from elsewhere.\n\nNext N>4, so u=N-m>5/3. If v<=sqrt(2), the third cut gives\n\n    N>=C(C-D)/(C+D).\n\nFor C>=C0,D<=lambda<17/7, this is greater than\nC0(C0-17/7)/(C0+17/7)=89359/22200>4. The monotonicities follow from\npartial_C[C(C-D)/(C+D)]=1-2D^2/(C+D)^2>0 for C>D, and\npartial_D=-2C^2/(C+D)^2<0.\n\nFor sqrt(2)<=v<9/5, suppose instead N<=4. Then L=C-N>=Lstar:=93/25.\nUse(22) at the virtual Lstar. Its denominator stays positive because\n\n    (Lstar-D)(D-2) <= (Lstar-2)^2/4\n                  =1849/2500<1.\n\nThe monotonicity R_c>0 in(23), with the actual cd<1, gives\nR(L,D)>=R(Lstar,D). Take rstar=16/C0^2, which lies in(0,1/3).\nAt D=lambda, using lambda<17/7,\n\n    C0*(Lstar/lambda-1)\n       >C0*(Lstar/(17/7)-1)=43618/10625>4.\n\nThus R(Lstar,lambda)>rstar. At D=14/5, direct substitution gives\n\n    R(Lstar,14/5)=2116/5185>1/3>rstar.\n\nEquations(24),(25) with L=Lstar show R(Lstar,D)>rstar for the whole\ninterval. Consequently C^2 R(L,D)>16>=N^2, contradicting(14).\nThis proves N>4 also on the higher-terminal branch.\n\nWe have therefore derived, for every old survivor in(1),\n\n    5/3<u<2, 1<=m<=7/3, c>0,\n    q=(u-1)/m in(0,1), 0<t<=N/C<1.                (27)\n\nThe last inequality is the third cut. Thus qt<1. All zero and small\nstep cases were handled before choosing a family; none was discarded.\nFor v<=sqrt(2) use the planar function in Section5. The two subgradient inequalities at x,x' give\n<g-g',y-y'>>=0 and hence ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. Thus g is1-Lipschitz.\nEvaluating the proximal objective for x' at y(x) gives its upper first\norder bound. Valid reduction, with no unjustified homogeneous conversion\n\nFrom (2) alone, cancellation of beta gives\n\n    Mv^2<=(1+u)delta+u alpha=delta+u(A-Mu),\n    R104^2>=R_0^2:=(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2.        (13)\n\nThe radius inequality follows from kappa<=u alpha+v beta. Completing\neach terminal square, using s,ell>=0 and alpha,beta>=0, gives\n\n    2s(Eu-alpha)+2ell(Ev-beta)-sigma(s^2+ell^2)\n       <=2E(su+ell v)-sigma(s^2+ell^2)\n       <=E^2(u^2+v^2)/sigma.\n\nSince the true numerator is positive,\n\n    V104<=E^2(u^2+v^2)/(sigma R_0^2).                   (14)\n\nThis is an upper relaxation. It neither replaces the actual static\nfunction mid-trajectory nor assumes the square-maximizing r is legal.\n\nFor completeness, on the subdomain0<=s<=1 the change beta'=0,\nkappa'=u alpha, keeping the other parameters and the word fixed, IS a\nlegal whole-instance improvement. The prefix r comparison remains\nautomatic. The new first q comparison is exactly (13); the new middle\nreserve is0. At every tail query, z increases by(0,beta): the zero and\np margins rise by ell beta, and the q margin rises by\nell beta+(u alpha+v beta-kappa)>=0. Its radius does not increase and\nits positive numerator rises by2ell beta. This legal conversion is\nnot asserted for s>1, where the prefix r comparison must also be paid.\nThe proof of (14) and all conclusions below do not need that conversion.\n\n## 6. New full middle-gradient norm versus true-radius comparison\n\nWe prove from scratch: for A,M,T>0 with T>=2A, u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,\n\n    delta=A-Mu-alpha>=0,\n    Mv^2<=delta+u(A-Mu),\n\none has\n\n    (u^2+v^2)/[(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2]\n       <=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.                 (15)\n\nThis controls the horizontal contribution that the former orthogonal\nargument did not need. It includes both nearest-minimizer branches.\n\nFirst suppose M<=A+2A^2/T. Put P=A-Mu and d=delta, so0<=d<=P.\nThe constraint gives M(u^2+v^2)<=Au+d. Direct expansion yields\n\n    A^2(T+d)^2-M T^2(Au+d)\n       =A P T^2+(2A^2T-MT^2)d+A^2d^2>=0.              (16)\n\nIf the coefficient of d is nonnegative, every term is nonnegative.\nIf it is negative, substitute d<=P in just that term; the result is\nat least P[(A-M)T^2+2A^2T]+A^2d^2>=0 by this regime's bound on M.\nDropping the second nonnegative radius square now proves the first\nalternative A^2/(M^2T^2) in (15). No saturation or endpoint limit is\nneeded in this regime.\n\nSuppose next M>A+2A^2/T. Then M>A. For fixed u,alpha put\n\n    P=A-Mu, d=P-alpha, k=1+u, c=uP,\n    x=v^2, c_0=u alpha/M, H_0=T+d.\n\nLegal v>0 forces P>0. The ratio on the left of (15) is\n\n    (u^2+x)/[H_0^2+M^2(x-c_0)_+^2/x].                  (17)\n\nOn x<=c_0 it increases. On x>=c_0, differentiation has the sign of\n\n    H_0^2-2M^2c_0-M^2u^2\n      +2M^2c_0^2/x+M^2u^2c_0^2/x^2.\n\nThe first three terms are at least\n\n    T^2-2Mu(A-Mu)-M^2u^2\n       =T^2-A^2+(A-Mu)^2>=0.\n\nThe branches agree at x=c_0. Therefore (17) is bounded by its value\nat the allowed endpoint x=(d+c)/M. There\nMx-u alpha=(1+u)d>=0, giving the bound\n\n    F_u(d)=(Au+d)/\n       { M[(T+d)^2+M(1+u)^2d^2/(d+c)] }, 0<=d<=P.     (18)\n\nThis saturation is only an upper relaxation; no final activity is assumed.\n\nWrite b_0=Au. For u>0, c>0, differentiation of (18) has the sign of\n\n    G_u(d)=(T+d)(T-d-2b_0)-M(1+u)^2 H_u(d),\n    H_u(d)=d[(b_0+c)d+2b_0c]/(d+c)^2.\n\nExplicitly\n\n    H_u'(d)=2c^2(d+b_0)/(d+c)^3>=0,\n    G_u'(d)=-2(d+b_0)-M(1+u)^2H_u'(d)<=0,\n    G_u(P)=(T+A)(T-A-2Au).                            (19)\n\nThe last identity follows by substituting P=A-Mu and d+c=P(1+u):\nthe subtracted term at P is Mu(A+P+2Au), which combined with the\nfirst product leaves exactly the displayed factorization.\n\nIn the present regime\n\n    u<=A/M<T/(T+2A)<=(T-A)/(2A).\n\nThe last inequality is equivalent to (T-2A)(T+A)>=0. Hence\nG_u(P)>=0. Because G_u is nonincreasing, G_u(d)>=G_u(P)>=0 throughout\n[0,P]; thus F_u(d) is nondecreasing. When u=0, its formula reduces to\nd/{M[(T+d)^2+Md]}, whose derivative has the sign T^2-d^2>0 for\n0<d<=A. Its limit at d=0 is0. These observations also handle all\nrelaxed endpoint degeneracies; the actual constraint always has v>0.\n\nIt follows that (18) is at most its alpha=0 value at d=P. Writing\nt=P in[0,A], that value is\n\n    [A^2+(M-A)t]/[M^2(T^2+(2T+A+M)t)].                 (20)\n\nThis is a quotient of positive affine functions of t. Its derivative\nhas the sign of\n\n    (T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)].\n\nIf nonpositive, the maximum is its value at t=0, namely A^2/(M^2T^2).\nIf nonnegative, the maximum is at t=A and equals\nA/{M[(T+A)^2+MA]}. In the latter case\n\n    M[(T+A)^2+MA]-A(T+A+M)^2\n       =(T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0,\n\nso the value at t=A is at most1/(T+A+M)^2. This proves (15).\nEvery relaxation, both radius branches, and the u=0 and boundary cases\nhave been retained. Combining (14) with (15) proves (6).\n\n## 7. Every original ruler pair, with its actual tail price\n\nFix the ORIGINAL enclosing m>=128, abbreviate a=a_m, c_r=c_m and\nrho=c_r-2, and put a_k=a c_r^k for the level-k checkpoint excess.\nThe exact mass recurrence and its solution are\n\n    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+a c_r^k,\n    M_k=[a/(c_r-2)]c_r^k+[1-a/(c_r-2)]2^k.              (21)\n\nThe inequalities sqrt(2)<10/7 and m>=128 give\n\n    12/5<lambda<c_r<49/20,\n    0<a<lambda-2<rho<=9/20,\n    M_k<=c_r^k, M_(k+1)<=c_r M_k, a_k<=rho M_k.         (22)\n\nFor the upper bound on c_r, use\nc_r<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20. For the bound on a, use\na=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda]<1/lambda=lambda-2. The remaining bounds follow\nfrom the positive two-term formula (21). In particular the term\n(a/rho)c_r^k alone proves a_k<=rho M_k.\n\nA level-k checkpoint has an immediately adjacent H_k before and after\nit. Its complete prefix and suffix masses plus1 are therefore at least\nM_k. For every first checkpoint in any pair, A/T<=rho<=9/20<1/2.\nThus T>=2A, as required by the new full-norm comparison.\n\nFor actual checkpoint indices i<j of levels k,l, respectively, put\n\n    R_pair=AE/(TM), q_pair=c_r rho^2.\n\nIf i precedes the entire H_l immediately before j, then T>=M_k and\nM>=M_l, so R_pair<=rho^2. This includes k>=l because such a level\ncannot occur inside H_l. Otherwise i lies inside that particular H_l,\nso k<l. Let x be1 plus i's local prefix mass inside H_l, and y=M.\nIts local adjacent copies imply x,y>=M_k, and the exact identity is\nx+y=M_l-a_k. As T>=x,\n\n    TM>=xy>=M_k(M_l-a_k-M_k).\n\nWrite theta=a_k/M_k<=rho and zeta=M_l/M_k>=2+theta. The latter bound\nfollows from l>=k+1 and M_(k+1)=2M_k+a_k. Since E<=rho M_l,\n\n    R_pair<=theta rho zeta/(zeta-1-theta)\n           <=theta rho(2+theta)<=rho^2(2+rho)=q_pair.\n\nThe middle function decreases in zeta for zeta>=2+theta, and the\nlast expression increases in theta>=0. This covers every actual pair.\nIn particular q_pair<=(49/20)(9/20)^2=3969/8000<1/2.\n\nPalindromicity applies this very same pair estimate in reverse and\ngives AE/(NM)<=q_pair. The local side bounds give A<=rho T and\nE<=rho N. Consequently\n\n    R_pair<=q_pair,\n    T<=q_pair N/R_pair,\n    M<=rho^2 N/R_pair.\n\nAll quantities divided by here are positive. The full physical word\nmass, including both checkpoints and every ordinary query, is exactly\n\n    M_m=T+M+N+A+E.\n\nMultiplying by R_pair and using the preceding bounds yields\n\n    R_pair<=min{q_pair,D_pair N/M_m},\n    D_pair=2(1+rho)q_pair+rho^2.                         (23)\n\nThe constants satisfy\n\n    q_pair D_pair<=2(29/20)(1/2)^2+(81/400)(1/2)\n                  =661/800,\n    D_pair/q_pair=2(1+rho)+1/c_r<=199/60<4.\n\nThe actual M_m is at least16: its word has2^m-1 steps, each greater\nthan1. Therefore N_0=q_pair M_m/D_pair>1. For1<=N<=N_0 use (23)\nand the increasing function N^2/(2N-1); for N>=N_0 use the other\nbranch and the decreasing function1/(2N-1). At their common crossover,\n\n    R_pair^2/(2N-1)<=q_pair D_pair/(2M_m-D_pair/q_pair).\n\nRelative to the SAME-word canonical Huber value, this gives\n\n    [R_pair^2/(2N-1)]/[1/(2M_m-1)]\n       <=(661/800)(2M_m-1)/(2M_m-199/60)\n       <=(661/800)*31/(32-199/60)=61473/68840.           (24)\n\nThe rational function decreases with M_m, so the final bound follows\nfrom M_m>=16. The original tail factor2N-1 was retained throughout.\n\n## 8. Complete SAME-word K, ray and applicable lens references\n\nFor accepted b98bb807d514e80d, EVERY single-cut term at a level-k step\nh=1+a_k has denominator\n\n    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)], S_total=M_m-1,\n\nwhere P is its COMPLETE physical prefix sum. Differentiation has the\nsign of2(S_total-h)-3P, so its minimum between first and last occurrence\nis at an endpoint. The first occurrence has prefix M_k-1 and suffix\nat least M_(m-1)-1; for the last these are reversed. The first endpoint\nis bounded using a_k<=rho M_k. For the other, use that x^2/(2x-1)\nincreases for x>=1 and M_k<=M_(m-1). Thus every cut is at most\nrho^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). Since M_m<=c_r M_(m-1),\n\n    Cut_i/[1/(2M_m-1)]<=rho^2(2c_r-1)\n                         <=3159/4000<gamma104.          (25)\n\nHere (2c_r x-1)/(2x-1)<=2c_r-1 for x>=1. Hence the COMPLETE maximum\nK_n(H_m) is its canonical Huber term1/(2M_m-1), not a sampled cut value.\n\nFor the complete F_ray_n reference use precisely the accepted recurrence\nand feasibility conditions of1fca3e5dd555cb4a. If a checkpoint selection\nhas at least two indices, its first two nonterminal magnitudes are\nm_0=1/T and m_1=A/(TM), and its old offset after the first cut is\nO_1=(T-1)m_0. Its budget just after the second cut is\n\n    E m_1-O_1=m_0[R_pair T-(T-1)]<m_0,\n\nbecause R_pair<=q_pair<1. With exactly two cuts, feasibility requires\nthis budget at least N m_0>=m_0. With more cuts, the next nonterminal\nmagnitude is this budget divided by a mass at least1. A nonpositive\nbudget already violates positivity; a positive budget still gives\nm_2<m_0, violating the required repeated-ray magnitude condition.\nThus no selection with two or more checkpoints is feasible. Zero and\nsingle checkpoint choices are exactly the complete K terms. We obtain\n\n    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1).                  (26)\n\nThe second term of (6) is the second actual checkpoint's single-cut\nterm, because its full prefix mass plus1 is T+A+M and suffix factor\nis2N-1. Apply (25) to it and (24) to the first term of (6). This proves\n(7) uniformly over every feasible tuple and every original partition.\n\nThe complete applicable reference from accepted dfde2fa326c908bf is\n\n    V_lens(h)=max{sup_(z in Lens_n)Gap_h(z),1/(1+2sum h)}.\n\nIt contains this SAME Huber term by definition. Thus\nV104<=gamma104 V_lens(H_m) for the entire family. This compares the\nfull Huber-augmented lens maximum without pretending to compute its\ncomplex component. It does not equate the universal lens with the larger\nword-dependent legal set D_h, and does not cover all nonlinear scalar\ntrajectories.\n\nC59/accepted e9a55f57461f253c remains confined to its ACTUAL H6 with\nepsilon1/6. Our m>=128 result is not a transfer of that witness to a\nchild word whose enclosing parameters changed. No new actual-H6 record\nis asserted. Accepted62a7d9329121fd75 remains the finite(2,2,1)\ncalibration; it is not used as an iteration or family-optimality premise.\n\n## 9. The original all-split B_n, without changing the denominator\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d, put C_n=1+S_circle(n),\nB_n=1+2S_bullet(n), C_0=B_0=1, and\n\n    C_n=max_(k+l+1=n)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n)G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.\n\nWe give the sharper elementary upper estimate needed here, rather than\nusing an unverified asymptotic of the benchmark. Set p_0=log_2(lambda)>1\nand f_0(t)=(1+t)^p_0-1 for t>0. The second derivative of log f_0(t)\nwith respect to log t is\n\n    p_0 t(1+t)^(p_0-2)\n      [(1+t)^p_0-1-p_0 t]/f_0(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive: it is0 at0 and has positive derivative for\nt>0. Convexity at log t and-log t gives\nf_0(t)f_0(1/t)>=f_0(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. Consequently\n\n    Omega(a,d)<=(a^(1/p_0)+d^(1/p_0))^p_0.\n\nIndeed the proposed right side X>=max(a,d) obeys(X-a)(X-d)>=2ad,\nwhere equality defines the positive root Omega; zero arguments follow\nby continuity. Induction over ALL splits in the ORIGINAL recurrence\ntherefore gives C_n<=(n+1)^p_0. The inequality G(a,d)<=4a+d follows\nby squaring sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=d+4a. A second all-split induction gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^p_0.                                     (27)\n\nFor an actual split k+l+1=n it uses (k+1)^p_0+l^p_0<=n^p_0;\nthis follows by integrating the increasing derivative of x^p_0 and\nincludes l=0. No reoptimized or selected denominator sequence appears.\n\nThe exact original enclosing-m mass in (21) satisfies\n\n    M_m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m\n      [a_m/(c_m-2)+(1-a_m/(c_m-2))(2/c_m)^m] ->exp(1).\n\nHere a_m/(c_m-2)->1 and2/c_m is uniformly bounded below1. Also\n(2^m-1)^p_0/lambda^m->1. Combining (7) and (27) gives (8).\nIts bound is strictly below1, since gamma104<1 and exp(1)>2. This\ndoes not assert convergence of the actual family supremum.\n\n## 10. What is decided, and what is not a legal inference\n\nThis joint draft decides the whole declared positive-quadrant endpoint\nfamily, with no restriction on the acute angle, no prescribed coordinate\nratio, no cap on its magnitude, no linkage between alpha and beta, all\nmiddle intercepts allowed by (2), and every original checkpoint pair.\nIt includes the orthogonal endpoint s=0. Its minimum set is the actual\ntilted set(9), and the closest-minimizer normalization was optimized\nexactly. Every physical query and every old-plane holding condition was\nretained. It does not merely improve one orthogonal calibration.\n\nThe alpha=0 and v-square saturations in Section6 are upper relaxations,\nnot assertions that a chosen final gradient remains legal after those\nchanges. The only actual conversion claimed is the beta/intercept\nconversion on0<=s<=1, explicitly proved in Section5. The all-positive\nclosure itself does not need that conversion, and does not silently\nextend its legality to s>1.\n\nIts exact remaining geometric escape is s<0, since then -2s alpha in\nthe terminal numerator is positive and the first inequality of Section5\ndoes not follow. Such final gradients are obtuse to p, and (9)'s\ncoordinatewise closest-minimizer argument can still hold; the failure\nis in the payoff charge, not automatically in the radius formula.\nNegative ell, a changed sign pattern for q, nonzero first/final relative\naffine costs, or more gradient changes/variation inside ordinary blocks\nare also outside the declared family. C99/C100 are not universal\nobstructions to any of these. No arbitrary-word minimax upper bound or\noriginal-target theorem has been proved.\n\n## 11. Actual obtuse static data outside the closure\n\nTo make the remaining s<0 hypothesis concrete, keep ANY actual\ntwo-checkpoint word and the same T,M,N,A,E. Define the following new\nparameters for this section only:\n\n    B=E/2, D=M+B, v=sqrt(A/(4D)),\n    alpha=min{A/2, (v/2)sqrt(B(2N+B))}>0,\n    delta=A-alpha, beta=Bv, u=0, kappa=0,\n    p=(1,0), q=(0,-v), z=(-alpha,Bv), r=z/(2N),\n    J_obt(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>,<r,y>},\n    F_obt(x)=min_y{J_obt(y)+||x-y||^2/2},\n    X_0=(T+delta,-Dv).                                  (28)\n\nHere p.r=-alpha/(2N)<0 and q,r are nonparallel. These are explicit\nreal vectors and a single function, not only a positive Gram matrix.\nThe direct regularity proof of Section2 applies verbatim to the finite\nmaximum in(28); none of its steps imposed a sign on r.\n\nAt the prefix ending query y=(delta,-Dv), p's height is delta>=A/2,\nq's is Dv^2=A/4, r's is negative, and zero's is0. Earlier prefix\nqueries add a nonnegative multiple of p, strengthening each comparison.\nAt the middle ending query y=(-alpha,-Bv), q's height is Bv^2>0,\np's is-alpha<0, and its margin over r is\n\n    [B(2N+B)v^2-alpha^2]/(2N)>0,\n\nbecause alpha^2<=B(2N+B)v^2/4. Earlier middle queries add a\nnonnegative multiple of q; its directional slope v^2 exceeds0,0,\nand q.r=-Bv^2/(2N). Thus every middle query is legal. The second\ncheckpoint sends the point exactly to z.\n\nAt any tail mass t in[0,N-1], the proposed proximal point is\n\n    y=[1-(1+t)/(2N)]z.\n\nIts bracket lies in[1/2,1-1/(2N)] and is positive. The r-plane has\npositive height, while p and q have negative heights. Hence every tail\nquery has gradient r. Identity(10) now proves every ordered row, with\nzero cost for all slopes; no remote-row condition is omitted.\n\nThe actual minimizer is uniquely0. In fact p.y<=0 forces y_1<=0,\nq.y<=0 forces y_2>=0, and r.y<=0 then forces both coordinates to0,\nsince both-alpha*y_1 and Bv*y_2 are nonnegative. Therefore\n\n    R_obt^2=(T+A-alpha)^2+D^2v^2,\n    V_obt=[(2N+1)/(4N^2)]\n           [alpha^2+B^2v^2]/[(T+A-alpha)^2+D^2v^2].       (29)\n\nThe terminal affine reserve is||z||^2/(4N)>0. The first and middle\nending reserves are delta and Bv^2, also positive. All optimum slacks\nare0, since every affine cost is0 and the true minimizer is0.\n\nFor appended total mass tau the proximal point is\n[1/2-tau/(2N)]z. Every plane comparison holds exactly until tau=N.\nAt tau=N all four planes meet at0. For tau>N the r-plane is negative\nand zero defeats it, so r cannot remain the gradient. The separate\nzero,p,q support-plane limits all equal N: each margin at the terminal\npoint is N times its positive rate of loss under motion-r. Thus this\nexample's exact paid continuation is\n\n    0<=tau<=N,\n    V_obt(tau)=V_obt(0)-2tau||r||^2/R_obt^2.             (30)\n\nThis is a real static realization on EVERY original word and at its\nactual n. It is not declared an improving adversary: the complete\nsame-word profile comparison of general obtuse endpoints remains OPEN.\nThe formulas alone are not that comparison. Its purpose is to certify\nthat the remaining negative-coordinate hypothesis has nonempty physical\ncontent; the quantitative decided deliverable is the full positive\nfamily domination(7)--(8), not an undecided optimization problem.\n\n### 11.1. An original-word sign counterexample, with its weakness priced\n\nChoose any two distinct level-0 checkpoints of the ORIGINAL H_m,\nm>=128, in (28). Their excesses are A=E=a_m, so B=A/2 and\n0<A<lambda-2<3/7<1/2. The ordinary blocks and all enclosing-m\nparameters stay exactly as prescribed. At this legal static instance,\nthe difference between the two sides of the FIRST sign-deletion\ninequality of Section5, before its square completion, is\n\n    -2s alpha-2ell beta=(alpha^2-B^2v^2)/N>0.           (31)\n\nTo prove strict positivity, alpha^2 is the minimum of\nA^2/4 and v^2 B(2N+B)/4. The first exceeds B^2v^2 because\n4D>A. The second exceeds B^2v^2 because2N>3B. Both are strict from\nD>=1,N>=1 and A<3/7. Thus dropping the assumption s>=0 in that\ndisplay really makes it false on original physical words. This does\nNOT refute the full bound(15), whose hypotheses do not mention s,\nor prove that the larger family beats the benchmark.\n\nIndeed this very counterexample is weaker than the complete same-word\nreferences. From D>=M>=1, alpha<=A/2 and A<1/2,\n\n    alpha^2+B^2v^2<=A^2/4+A^3/(16D)<=9A^2/32,\n    R_obt^2>=T^2+AM/4,\n    V_obt<=27A^2/(128N R_obt^2).\n\nSince M_m=T+M+N+2A, each term is bounded separately to give\n\n    M_m/(N R_obt^2)<=2+4/A+2A.\n\nFor example the middle-mass term is at most4/(NA)<=4/A, the prefix\nand tail terms are each at most1, and the excess term is at most2A.\nIt follows that\n\n    (2M_m-1)V_obt\n       <=(27/32)(A^3+A^2+2A)\n       <=(27/32)(384/343)=324/343<1.                    (32)\n\nThe polynomial increases for A>=0, and its value at A=3/7 is384/343.\nBy (26), this proves V_obt<=(324/343)K_n(H_m)\n=(324/343)F_ray_n(H_m), and the Huber-augmented lens also dominates.\nBy (27) and the original mass limit, its original-B limsup is at most\n2(324/343)/exp(1)<1. This is a same-word counterexample to one proposed\nproof shortcut, explicitly NOT an improving adversary. General obtuse\nendpoint parameters and other checkpoint levels are not closed by (32).\n\n## 12. Literature grounding and resource accounting\n\nThe search program was active-program/mutation: alter endpoint angle\nwithin the known finite affine-envelope regime, with the missing\nmechanism a full-gradient true-radius estimate on the original word.\nMatlas returned arXiv2205.12838 Lemma5.8,2105.13913 Proposition3.1,\nand2111.07720 Lemma5.1, all concerning constrained hull/Frank--Wolfe\ngeometry. Their compact-domain conditional-gradient assumptions do not\nsupply this unconstrained long-step payoff comparison; none was imported.\nThe targeted obtuse-example follow-up returned arXiv2111.14069 Lemma6\n(negative-curvature saddle escape, incompatible with our convex setting)\nand2112.02525 Theorem3.3 (a generic constrained stationarity statement).\nNeither supplies or is used in the original-word counterexample.\n\nWeb retrieval recovered Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,\n\"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance\nof First-order Methods\",arXiv1502.05666. The known exact local file\ndownloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt was read at Section2.5,\nTheorem4,its proof/construction,and Corollary1. Its finite Euclidean\nsmooth-convex definition specializes to smoothness1 here; the needed\nregularity and all directed inequalities were rederived directly above,\nwith accepted1252b8823bb5a290 fixing the project row convention.\nNo new external theorem is a proof dependency. This direction remains\ninside that previously audited finite-envelope regime and needs no\nadditional machinery or unexamined external definition.\n\nAll mathematics was symbolic/textual. No numerical evaluation, solver,\nmachine algebra, replay, formal verifier, installation, consult, native\nsubagent or new fact was used. The empty initial memory query returned\ncount0; the exact shared master_guidance file and targeted searches\nrecovered the steering. This is a precise mechanism failure under the\nbounded-allocation guidance, not a claim that the requested seed was\nfound or proved impossible.\n\nOnly accepted facts04219554e41d9750 (Section10, the full primitive\nidentity) and1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation orientation and realization)\nare correctness inputs. Every new ingredient is proved within this\nsingle draft. No unverified earlier triangle or scalar result is used\nas a premise. In particular defective43a616da7da451c7 and its unchecked\nglobal reductions are neither used nor silently repaired.\n\n## 2. Original certificate and primitive energy\n\nUse all original indices0,1,2,3,4. Let Lambda_ij>=0 on all twenty\ndistinct finite pairs and Lambda_ii=0. Let e_all be the all-ones column,\nT_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i and0 for t>i, and set\n\n    L_flow=diag(Lambda e_all)-Lambda,\n    omega=(-2u,-2w,-2z,-2v,2(C-1))^T,\n    Dprim=diag(u(2-u),w(2-w),z(2-z),v(2-v),-(C-1)(C+1)),\n    M=Dprim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.\n\nThe sought certificate requires L_flow^T e_all=omega and M positive\nsemidefinite on ALL of R5. No formal coordinate or coefficient is\nremoved. For actual smoothness-one GD data define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)\n         +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-(C-1)C||g_4||^2.\n\nThe accepted identity is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>.\n\nEvery actual convex1-smooth trajectory has Q_ij<=0. Thus a negative\nactual V_h excludes the entire original certificate, regardless of\nrank, support, or coefficients. This is the only inference about the\noriginal cone used below.\n\nFor the saturated values\n\n    f_i^0=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n\ndirect expansion gives\n\n    Q_ij^0=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>,\n    V_h(f^0)=||x_0||^2-||x_4+(C-1)g_4||^2.           (1)\n\nFor the second identity, the sum of\n2h_i<x_i,g_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2 telescopes to\n||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2. The remaining terms are\n-2(C-1)<x_4,g_4>-(C-1)^2||g_4||^2.\n\n## 3. The two prescribed marginal triangles\n\nDefine positive constants\n\n    Kp=a^2 b+(1-alpha)(u+w),\n    Kt=c^2 d+(1-gamma)(z+v),\n    R=a^2 b/Kp, tau=c^2 d/Kt, Tnorm=R*tau.           (2)\n\nThen0<R<1 and0<tau<1. The following alternative expressions will\nalso be needed:\n\n    Kp=w+1-a(w^2-2),\n    Kt=v+1-c(v^2-2),\n    Kt-c=(v+1)(1-gamma).                            (3)\n\nFor example, Kp=a^2 b+a+w+1-ab(a+w+1)\n=a+w+1-ab(w+1)=w+1-a((b+1)^2-2).\nThe same substitution with c,d gives the second identity; subtracting\nc gives the third.\n\nLet E,P,Z be vectors with the prescribed marginal inner products\n\n    ||E||^2=1,\n    <E,P>=||P||^2=R,\n    <P,Z>=||Z||^2=Tnorm=R*tau.                     (4)\n\nOnly the cross inner product <E,Z> is not prescribed. Write\n\n    <E,Z>=R*beta.\n\nDefine the two intermediate gradients\n\n    Agrad=-(aE+P)/w, Bgrad=-(cP+Z)/v,\n\nand the actual chronological transcript\n\n    x=(E,-aE,P,-cP,Z),\n    g=(E,Agrad,P,Bgrad,Z).                          (5)\n\nAll four GD updates hold exactly: E-uE=-aE,\n-aE-wAgrad=P, P-zP=-cP, and-cP-vBgrad=Z.\nThe only nonzero target normals are\n\n    n=x_1-g_1=(P-alpha E)/w,\n    m=x_3-g_3=(Z-gamma P)/v.                        (6)\n\nThe constants in(2) were chosen to give the two local equalities\n\n    <Agrad,n>=<P,n>=(1-alpha)R/w,\n    <Bgrad,m>=<Z,m>=(1-gamma)Tnorm/v.               (7)\n\nHere is the first equality explicitly. Expansion of Agrad and n gives\n\n    w^2<Agrad,n>=a*alpha+(alpha-a)R-R.\n\nSubtract w(1-alpha)R. The result is\na^2 b-[w+1-a(w^2-2)]R=0 by(2),(3).\nThe same expansion with initial squared norm R, parameters c,d,\nand terminal squared norm R*tau proves the second equality.\n\nIndividually, the first triangle has vertices E,Agrad,P. Its only\nnonzero query normal is n. Equation(7) gives equality at P, while\n\n    <E-Agrad,n>=-alpha(1-R)/w<0.\n\nAlso aE+wAgrad+P=0 with positive coefficients. It therefore contains\nzero and gives a legal static projection trajectory for the first\ntwo steps. The second triangle has vertices P,Bgrad,Z, contains zero\nbecause cP+vBgrad+Z=0, and its only nonzero query normal is m. Its\nremaining comparison is\n\n    <P-Bgrad,m>=-gamma(R-Tnorm)/v<0.\n\nThus both marginal trajectories are legal for every parameter in the\nstated phase. Their joint legality is a separate question.\n\n## 4. Every original directed row and every optimum row\n\nAssign saturated values f_i^0 as in Section2. Targets0,2,4 have zero\nnormal, so all twelve off-diagonal rows into those targets are zero.\nThe eight remaining original finite rows, with source first, are\n\n    Q_01^0=-alpha(1-R)/w,\n    Q_21^0=0,\n    Q_31^0=R[alpha*beta-tau-(c+v)(1-alpha)]/(v*w),\n    Q_41^0=R[tau-alpha*beta-(1-alpha)]/w,\n\n    Q_03^0=R[beta-gamma-(1-gamma)tau]/v,\n    Q_13^0=R[-a*beta+gamma(1+a)\n                        -(1+w(1-gamma))tau]/(w*v),\n    Q_23^0=-gamma R(1-tau)/v,\n    Q_43^0=0.                                      (8)\n\nFor example,\n<Bgrad,n>=[-c(1-alpha)R-(Tnorm-alpha R beta)]/(vw).\nSubtracting <Agrad,n> from(7) gives Q_31^0. Also\n<Agrad,m>=[-a(R beta-gamma R)-(Tnorm-gamma R)]/(wv);\nsubtracting <Bgrad,m> gives Q_13^0. The other displayed rows follow\ndirectly from(6),(7). This checks all twenty original rows.\n\nInclude x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Every Q_(i,star)^0 is zero.\nThe only nonzero Q_(star,i)^0 are\n\n    Q_(star,1)^0=-(1-alpha)R/w,\n    Q_(star,3)^0=-(1-gamma)R*tau/v,\n\nand these are negative. Thus the optimum constraints add no omitted\nrestriction. The entire analysis is on the actual chronological word.\n\n## 5. Exact covariance interval, attained in dimension at most three\n\nPut\n\n    S=sqrt((1-R)*tau*(1-tau)/R),\n    Lp=(tau-1+alpha)/alpha,\n    Up=[tau+(c+v)(1-alpha)]/alpha,\n    Lt=[gamma(1+a)-(1+w(1-gamma))*tau]/a,\n    Ut=gamma+(1-gamma)*tau.                         (9)\n\nThe COMPLETE necessary and sufficient condition for this family to\ngive a legal joint transcript is\n\n    max(Lp,Lt,tau-S) <= min(Up,Ut,tau+S).           (10)\n\nFirst, the Gram matrix of E,P,Z is\n\n    [ 1       R       R beta ]\n    [ R       R       R tau  ]\n    [ R beta  R tau   R tau  ].\n\nAfter eliminating the middle coordinate, its remaining quadratic is\nthe two-by-two matrix with diagonal1-R and R*tau*(1-tau), and\noff-diagonal R(beta-tau). Both diagonals are positive. Hence full\nGram positivity is exactly |beta-tau|<=S. Equation(8) gives precisely\nLp<=beta<=Up and Lt<=beta<=Ut. This proves necessity of(10).\n\nFor sufficiency select any beta in(10). In an orthonormal coordinate\nsystem E1,E2,E3 set\n\n    P=sqrt(R)*E1,\n    E=sqrt(R)*E1+sqrt(1-R)*E2,\n    Z=sqrt(R)*tau*E1+R(beta-tau)/sqrt(1-R)*E2\n      +sqrt(R*tau*(1-tau)-R^2(beta-tau)^2/(1-R))*E3. (11)\n\nThe radicand is nonnegative by(10). Formula(11) has exactly(4) and\n<E,Z>=R beta, including either equality endpoint of the Gram interval.\nEquation(8) proves every row. Let K be the convex hull of\nE,Agrad,P,Bgrad,Z. It contains zero by the first relation in Section3.\nDefine ONE static function\n\n    F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.           (12)\n\nCompleting the square identifies its unique maximizing g with the\nEuclidean projection onto K. The normal inequalities(8) imply that\neach g_i is that projection at x_i. In particular F_K(x_i)=f_i^0.\nAdding two projection inequalities gives\n<x-y,P_K(x)-P_K(y)> >= ||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2, so P_K is1-Lipschitz.\nThe two maximizing inequalities give\n0<=F_K(y)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),y-x>\n <=<P_K(y)-P_K(x),y-x><=||y-x||^2.\nThus F_K is differentiable with gradient P_K, which is1-Lipschitz.\nIt is convex as a maximum of affine functions and finite because K\nis compact. Since0 is in K, F_K>=0=F_K(0). It has a genuine minimizer.\nThis proves sufficiency and static attainment without an external\nunverified interpolation result. No normalized objective error or\nclaim of a common function for all words is made.\n\nOne useful equivalent form of(10) follows from\n\n    Lp<tau<Up,       tau<Ut.\n\nIf Lt<=tau, beta=tau is legal. If Lt>tau, the full condition is\n\n    Lt<=Up, Lt<=Ut, Lt<=tau+S.                      (13)\n\nThe other lower endpoints are then inactive. These conclusions follow\nbecause tau-Lp=(1-alpha)(1-tau)/alpha>0 and\nUp-tau=(1-alpha)(tau+c+v)/alpha>0. No interval branch is suppressed.\n\n## 6. Cross-stage obstruction and the failure of orthogonal gluing\n\nEven before testing the Gram matrix, the two original rows into target3\nforce Lt<=Ut. Substitution from(9) and then(3) gives\n\n    Lt<=Ut\n iff gamma <= [1+(w+a)(1-gamma)]*tau\n iff Kt <= c[1+(w+a)(1-gamma)]\n iff 1+v <= c(w+a).                               (14)\n\nIn the middle equivalence gamma=cd>0 and tau=c*gamma/Kt.\nFor the last one subtract c and use Kt-c=(v+1)(1-gamma)>0.\nConsequently c(w+a)<1+v makes this entire joint family EMPTY, with\nevery transverse covariance and every ambient dimension allowed.\nIt does not make the original primitive cone empty.\n\nThe direct orthogonal concatenation of the transverse directions\ncorresponds to beta=tau. Thus ALL five deltas are the same constant.\n\nThe primitive energy uses only the differences f_i-f_4. Every legal\nfinite value assignment therefore has exactly the same energy. This\nargument uses only the full finite rows; imposing any further optimum\nrows cannot create an additional value assignment or lower the energy.\nConversely(12) attains the displayed values with a genuine minimizer.\n\n## 8.
